# Simulation principles

Contents: 1. Simulation kinds · 2. Simulated time · 3. Fixed step · 4. Integration and stability · 5. Determinism · 6. Fidelity · 7. Verification, validation, accreditation · 8. Pitfalls · Sources

## 1. Simulation kinds (discrete-event, continuous, hybrid)

A model describes a system. A simulation imitates the behavior of that system. [S14]

Three kinds of simulation exist.

- Discrete-event simulation: the state changes only at separate points of simulated time. Each change is an event. The clock jumps from one event to the next earliest event. Future events wait in a list sorted by time. [S1]
- Continuous simulation: the state changes all the time. A solver computes the continuous states by numerical integration, one step after the other. [S2]
- Hybrid simulation: both kinds in one program. A scheduler calls the continuous models at a fixed period and runs events between the steps. [S3] [S5]

A training simulator mixes both kinds. Physical processes are simulated continuously, in real time. Malfunctions and instructor commands occur at chosen times or on events. [S10]

Use these terms in code and comments. Use one term for one idea.

| Term | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| model | The description of a system. [S14] |
| simulation | The imitation of the behavior of a system. [S14] |
| event | A happening that changes the state at one point of simulated time. [S1] |
| step | One advance of simulated time by the step size of the solver. [S2] |
| frame | One cycle of the scheduler. The scheduler compares simulated time with the wall clock at the end of each frame. [S4] |

## 2. Simulated time and wall-clock time (simulation time)

Simulated time is the time inside the simulation. Wall-clock time is the time of the host computer. They are two different clocks. [S3] [S5]

- The scheduler owns simulated time. Models receive it from the scheduler. [S3]
- Count simulated time as an integer number of ticks. A floating-point number of seconds has roundoff error at large values. [S3]
- Real time: at the end of each frame, the scheduler waits until the wall clock reaches simulated time. [S4]
- Underrun: the frame ends early and the scheduler waits. This is the normal case. [S4]
- Overrun: the frame takes longer than its period. The simulation runs slower than real time. The scheduler starts the next frame at once to catch up. [S4]
- Faster than real time: without the wait, the simulation runs as fast as the computer allows. [S4]
- Slow time and fast time: a training simulator can also run at a fraction or a multiple of real time. [S10]
- Freeze: simulated time stops. The program keeps running. Only work tied to the wall clock goes on. [S3] [S5]
- Save and restore: a saved state keeps simulated time. It does not keep wall-clock time. [S5]

Rule: a model never reads the wall clock. It uses only the simulated time and the step that the scheduler gives.

## 3. Fixed step and variable step (simulation time step)

A fixed-step solver uses the same step size from the start to the end of the run. A variable-step solver changes the step size during the run: smaller at an event or when the state changes fast, larger when the state changes slowly. [S2]

A real-time simulator uses a fixed step.

- Methods that change the time step do not suit real-time execution. [S11]
- Generated code that is deployed can use only a fixed-step solver. [S2]
- A fixed step gives the same result on every run. A step that varies does not. [S6]

A smaller step gives more accurate results and costs more computing time. [S2]

Wrong:

```csharp
// The step comes from the wall clock: it changes on every call.
double now = stopwatch.Elapsed.TotalSeconds;
model.Step(now - previous);
previous = now;
```

Right:

```csharp
// Fixed step. Simulated time is an integer count of ticks (1 tick = 1 microsecond).
const long StepTicks = 10_000;                      // 10 ms
const double StepSeconds = StepTicks / 1_000_000.0;

long simTicks = 0;
while (running)
{
    model.Step(StepSeconds);                 // always the same step
    simTicks += StepTicks;                   // integer time: no roundoff
    if (realTime)
    {
        realTimeClock.WaitUntil(simTicks);   // wait for the wall clock; skip to run faster
    }
}
```

The display rate can differ from the step rate. The display then interpolates between the last two states. [S6]

## 4. Integration and stability (simulation numerical integration)

A continuous model gives the derivatives of its states. An integrator computes the next states from them. Keep the two apart: one function for derivatives, one for integration. [S15]

- Common fixed-step integrators: Euler (1 derivative evaluation per step), Runge-Kutta 2 (2 evaluations), Runge-Kutta 4 (4 evaluations). More evaluations cost more time per step. [S15]
- Stability: with an explicit integrator, a step that is too large makes the computed values grow without bound. [S8]
- Stiff model: a model with a very fast part and a slow part. An explicit integrator then needs a very small step for the whole run. An implicit integrator stays stable with larger steps, but it solves an equation at each step. [S8]

Check the step size this way: [S9]

1. Run the simulation.
2. Run it again with a smaller step or a tighter tolerance.
3. Compare the two results.
4. If they do not differ significantly, the solution has converged. Keep the larger step.

Keep a step that passes this check and that the computer runs within its frame. [S4] [S9]

## 5. Determinism and reproducibility (simulation determinism)

A deterministic simulation gives the same outputs from the same initial state and the same inputs. Replay of recorded inputs then gives exactly the same result. [S7]

Follow these rules.

1. Use a fixed step. [S6]
2. Call the models in the same order on every step. [S4]
3. Never use wall-clock time in a model. Wall-clock time is not part of the saved state. [S3] [S5]
4. Never let the number of solver iterations depend on the speed of the processor. [S7]
5. Use stable numerical methods. An unstable method amplifies small differences. [S7]

Wrong:

```csharp
// Wall-clock time inside a model: different on every run, lost at restore.
pump.StartedAt = DateTime.UtcNow;
```

Right:

```csharp
// Simulated time only, given by the scheduler.
pump.StartedAtTicks = simTicks;
```

Limit: the same program on the same machine is reproducible. Another compiler, another processor architecture, or a debug build instead of a release build can change floating-point results. Do not expect bit-identical results across machines. [S7]

## 6. Fidelity and simulator classes (simulation fidelity)

Fidelity covers how specific to the real unit the simulator is, and how deep and accurate its models are. The training objectives set the level of fidelity that is needed. Higher fidelity costs more. [S10]

Simulators are named by their scope.

| Type | Scope |
| --- | --- |
| Full-scope simulator | Replica consoles and panels, and detailed models of the systems the operator works with. [S11] |
| Part-task simulator | One part of the operations or one phenomenon, often modeled more accurately. [S10] |
| Basic principle simulator | The fundamental physical processes. Auxiliary systems may be left out. [S10] |
| Plant analyser | Detailed study of complex transients. It need not run in real time. [S10] |

Maritime training simulators have classes: A (full mission), B (multi-task), C (limited task), S (special tasks). The standard is DNV-ST-0033. [S17]

Simulations are also named by who and what is real. [S16]

| Kind | People | Systems |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Live | Real | Real |
| Virtual | Real | Simulated |
| Constructive | Simulated | Simulated |

A training simulator with a real operator is a virtual simulation. [S16]

## 7. Verification, validation and accreditation (simulation VV&A)

- Verification: the program implements the model correctly. [S12] [S14]
- Validation: the results are accurate enough for the intended use. [S12] [S14]
- Accreditation: an official decision that the model is acceptable for a specific use. [S13]
- Credibility: the trust that users can put in the results. [S14]

State the accuracy that is required before you test. [S12]

These validation techniques work well as automated tests. [S13]

| Technique | What it checks |
| --- | --- |
| Comparison to other models | The results match a valid analytic model or a valid simulation model. |
| Degenerate test | The model behaves logically when a parameter takes an extreme value. |
| Extreme condition test | The outputs stay plausible for an unlikely combination of inputs. |
| Sensitivity analysis | The outputs respond to a change of input as the real system would. |
| Trace | One entity is followed through the model to check the logic. |

A training simulator is accepted by tests against reference data of the real plant. Each test has acceptance criteria, initial conditions and expected operator actions. The tests run in stages: before the factory test, at the factory, then on site. [S11]

## 8. Pitfalls (simulation principles)

- A job period that is not a whole number of ticks: the period is rounded and the job drifts. Example: a 128 Hz job with ticks of 1 microsecond has a period of 7812.5 ticks. Choose a tick that divides every period: with 10,000,000 ticks per second, the period is exactly 78,125 ticks. [S3]
- A frame overrun: the simulation falls behind real time. Set a limit on overruns and stop cleanly when it is passed. [S4]
- A loop that falls behind and runs more steps to catch up becomes slower again. Cap the number of steps per frame and accept a slower simulation. [S6]
- A step driven by the wall clock: results change from run to run, and a large step can make a model blow up. [S6]
- A stiff model with an explicit integrator: the values blow up, or the step must be very small. [S8]
- A method that changes the time step in a real-time loop: it does not suit real-time execution. [S11]
- Wall-clock time stored in the state: it is lost at restore. [S5]
- Bit-identical results expected across machines or builds: floating-point results can differ. [S7]

# Stimulation, emulation and hardware in the loop

Contents: 1. Four ways · 2. Constraints · Sources

## 1. Four ways to bring a real system into a simulator

| Way | Definition (paraphrase) |
| --- | --- |
| Stimulation | The real hardware and software of the plant run in the simulator, fed by the models [S1] |
| Emulation | The software of the real system is ported into the simulator environment and behaves as the reference [S1] |
| Simulation | Models written in the simulator environment reproduce the system within set functional limits [S1] |
| Hardware in the loop | A real embedded device is tested against a simulation of what it controls; emulated sensors and actuators make the interface [S2] |

## 2. Constraints of real or emulated systems

- A stimulated or emulated system must itself support freeze, run and backtrack, or a software layer must add them: a risk for functions and for the schedule. [S1]
- A hardware-in-the-loop bench needs a deterministic environment, with low latency and low jitter. [S2]
- In a virtualized full flight simulator, the motion computer keeps a hard real-time system and is not virtualized. [S3]
- Choose simulation when freeze, snapshot and replay matter more than an exact copy of the control software; choose stimulation or emulation when the training is about that software itself. Ask the team which one the project uses.

## Sources

- [S1] IAEA, "Guidelines for upgrade and modernization of nuclear power plant training simulators", IAEA-TECDOC-1500, 2006, <https://www-pub.iaea.org/MTCD/Publications/PDF/te_1500_web.pdf>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S2] Wikipedia, "Hardware-in-the-loop simulation", <https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hardware-in-the-loop_simulation>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY-SA 4.0, summarized.
- [S3] Arbic, Boivin, Giannias, "Leveraging Data Center Architectures for Full Flight Simulators", I/ITSEC 2024, paper 24155, <https://s3.amazonaws.com/amz.xcdsystem.com/44ECEE4F-033C-295C-BAE73278B7F9CA1D_abstract_File18333/StageTwoPaper_24155_0704044225.pdf>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.

# Real-time simulation in containers and Kubernetes

Contents: 1. Rule · 2. Published experience · 3. What to write · Sources

## 1. Rule of deployment (stepped core)

Keep the stepped core in one fixed-step process, as simple as possible; let out only data already computed. Split into services what surrounds it (instructor station, scenarios, snapshots, streaming), never the computation loop. Two published experiences go this way; none measured it on a .NET stack. [S3] [S5]

## 2. Published experience (containers and real time)

| Source | Context | Finding | Limit |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| NATO MSG-136, 2019 [S1] | "M&S as a Service": HLA federates in containers | The default Docker overlay network does not pass multicast; containers run unchanged under Docker and Kubernetes | No latency figure in the part read |
| Linutronix, 2023 [S2] | Real-time Linux kernel (PREEMPT_RT), cyclictest, one 4-core machine | Container latency almost equal to bare metal (about 23 µs), virtual machines higher (54 µs at most); needs extra Linux capabilities, the Guaranteed class and one physical core per real-time container | One machine; vendor blog |
| CAE, I/ITSEC 2024 [S3] | Virtualized full flight simulator, two containerized applications | Default settings: near 10 % of 100 Hz frames start 1 ms early or late; with exclusive cores, memory alignment, real-time kernel and fixed priority: as bare metal | Relaxed isolation; motion computer not virtualized; no Kubernetes |
| CAE, I/ITSEC 2025 [S4] | Distributed training on Kubernetes, Kafka, HLA and DIS gateways, Angular clients | Direct HLA about 3 ms; through messaging about 9 ms more; each gateway about 2 ms; almost every integration needed field-by-field fixes | Vendor measures |
| Kratzke, Siegfried, 2021 [S5] | Cloud-native simulation | Smaller composable services gain; moving an existing model as it is can raise costs | Read from the abstract |

## 3. What to write for the stepped core (Kubernetes)

- A Pod of class "Guaranteed": requests equal to limits, CPU as a whole number; the `static` CPU manager policy on the node gives it exclusive cores. [S6] See `docs/kubernetes/resources-realtime.md`.
- No multicast across the default container network: use host networking for that Pod or a unicast transport, by decision of the team. [S1]
- Measure frame overruns in the real cluster before any conclusion.

## Sources

- [S1] NATO STO MSG-136, "Modelling and Simulation as a Service, Volume 4: Experimentation Report", 2019, <https://apps.dtic.mil/sti/pdfs/AD1079102.pdf>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S2] Linutronix, "Containers and Realtime, is that possible? Even with Kubernetes?", 2023-07-06, <https://www.linutronix.de/blog/Containers-and-Realtime-is-that-possible-Even-with-Kubernetes>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S3] Arbic, Boivin, Giannias, "Leveraging Data Center Architectures for Full Flight Simulators", I/ITSEC 2024, <https://s3.amazonaws.com/amz.xcdsystem.com/44ECEE4F-033C-295C-BAE73278B7F9CA1D_abstract_File18333/StageTwoPaper_24155_0704044225.pdf>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S4] Krupp, Koch, Labas, Freeman, Bisaccia, "Achieving Distributed Training Through MSaaS", I/ITSEC 2025, <https://s3.amazonaws.com/amz.xcdsystem.com/44ECEE4F-033C-295C-BAE73278B7F9CA1D_abstract_File20450/PaperStage3FinalEdits_25271_0829031500.pdf>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S5] Kratzke, Siegfried, "Towards cloud-native simulations", Journal of Defense Modeling and Simulation 18(1), 2021, <https://econpapers.repec.org/RePEc:sae:joudef:v:18:y:2021:i:1:p:39-58>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S6] Kubernetes, "Control CPU Management Policies on the Node", <https://kubernetes.io/docs/tasks/administer-cluster/cpu-management-policies/>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.

# Training simulator architecture

Contents: 1. The parts · 2. Three references · 3. Order in a frame · Sources

## 1. The parts of a training simulator

The instructor station commands the executive; the executive calls the models; everything that shows, records or links hardware goes through the shared data.

```mermaid
flowchart LR
  IOS["Instructor station"] -->|"freeze, run, scenario, malfunctions"| EXE["Executive: scheduler and simulated time"]
  EXE -->|"calls at each step"| MOD["Component models"]
  MOD -->|"outputs"| BUS["Shared data"]
  BUS -->|"inputs"| MOD
  EXE -->|"save and restore"| SNAP["State snapshots"]
  BUS --> REC["Recorder and replay"]
  BUS --> GW["Streaming gateway"]
  GW --> HMI["Web interface: synoptics"]
  HMI -->|"operator actions"| BUS
```

- The "data bus" is a table of named variables: written by the models, read by the instructor station, the consoles, the recorder and the gateways. Three references describe it under three names. [S1] [S2] [S4]
- The web interface reads published data; it never calls a model.

## 2. Three references for the same parts (IAEA, ECSS SMP, NASA Trick)

| Part | Nuclear training (IAEA) | Space (ECSS SMP) | NASA Trick |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Core | Operating system and simulator executive; their scan and transfer rates size the I/O [S1] | Simulation environment and its services: logger, time keeper, scheduler, event manager, resolver, link registry [S2] | Executive and scheduler; real-time sync apart [S3] |
| Models | Models of the plant systems [S1] | Components that implement the model interface, in a tree [S2] | C/C++ functions grouped in simulation objects, each with a job class [S4] |
| Shared data | Model variables, open to debug tools [S1] | Published fields with state, input and output flags [S2] | Variable server for network clients; data recording [S4] |
| Instructor station | Instructor station software [S1] | Outside the part read | Control panel, a client of the variable server [S4] |
| Life cycle | — | Building, connecting, initialising, standby, executing, storing, restoring, reconnecting, exiting, aborting [S2] | Initialisation, run, freeze, shutdown [S3] |

## 3. Order of calls in a frame (Trick)

Trick calls its job classes in a fixed order in each frame: top of frame, integration loop, environment, sensors, scheduled jobs, effectors, logging, time advance, end of frame. [S3] [S4]

Keep one fixed order in the simulator too: inputs (operator and instructor), models, outputs (shared data, recorder, gateways).

## Sources

- [S1] IAEA, "Guidelines for upgrade and modernization of nuclear power plant training simulators", IAEA-TECDOC-1500, 2006, <https://www-pub.iaea.org/MTCD/Publications/PDF/te_1500_web.pdf>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S2] ECSS, "Simulation modelling platform", ECSS-E-ST-40-07C Rev.1, 2025-08-05, <https://ecss.nl/wp-content/uploads/2025/08/ECSS-E-ST-40-07C-Rev.1(5August2025).pdf>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S3] NASA, Trick documentation, "Executive Scheduler", <https://nasa.github.io/trick/documentation/simulation_capabilities/Executive-Scheduler>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S4] Penn, Lin, "The Trick Simulation Toolkit", NASA NTRS 20150021786, <https://ntrs.nasa.gov/api/citations/20150021786/downloads/20150021786.pdf>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.

# Simulation time and the scheduler loop

Contents: 1. Rules · 2. Ticks and rates · 3. The loop · 4. Overrun, freeze and time scale · Sources

## 1. Rules of simulation time

- A model advances only by its step. It never reads the clock of the machine. [S2] [S5]
- The step is fixed and comes from the scheduler. [S1] [S3]
- The executive tracks only simulated time; a separate part ties it to the wall clock for real time. [S2]
- Without that tie, the simulation runs as fast as it can. [S3]
- Four times exist: simulation time, epoch time, mission time and the wall clock ("Zulu"); the wall clock is never saved nor restored. [S5]

## 2. Ticks and rates (simulated time in integers)

- Count simulated time as an integer number of ticks, not as floating-point seconds: a double loses precision as the value grows. [S2]
- Every task period must be a whole number of ticks, else it is rounded and drifts: 128 Hz is 7,812.5 ticks of one microsecond, but exactly 78,125 ticks with 10 million ticks per second. [S2]
- Choose the tick so that every rate of the simulator divides it.

## 3. The scheduler loop (fixed step)

Advance only by fixed steps; keep the rest of the elapsed time in an accumulator; cap the steps per frame against the "spiral of death"; interpolate the display between the two last states. [S1]

```csharp
public sealed class Scheduler(IReadOnlyList<IModel> models, long stepTicks, TimeProvider clock)
{
    private long simulatedTicks;
    private long accumulatorTicks;
    private const int MaxStepsPerFrame = 5;

    public long SimulatedTicks => simulatedTicks;
    public bool IsFrozen { get; set; }
    public double TimeScale { get; set; } = 1.0;

    public void RunFrame(TimeSpan elapsed)
    {
        if (IsFrozen)
        {
            return;
        }
        accumulatorTicks += (long)(elapsed.Ticks * TimeScale);
        int steps = 0;
        while (accumulatorTicks >= stepTicks && steps < MaxStepsPerFrame)
        {
            foreach (IModel model in models)
            {
                model.Step(stepTicks);
            }
            simulatedTicks += stepTicks;
            accumulatorTicks -= stepTicks;
            steps++;
        }
    }
}
```

- The models are called in a fixed order at every step; a changing order breaks determinism. [S3]
- `TimeProvider` is the clock of the loop only; models never see it. The tick of this example is the .NET tick (100 ns).

## 4. Overrun, freeze and time scale (scheduler)

- A frame that ends early waits: the normal case. A frame that ends late (overrun) puts the simulation behind real time and the next frame starts at once; past a limit, stop cleanly. [S3]
- Freeze stops simulated time while the program runs: models are not called, the display stays alive. [S2] [S4]
- The instructor station changes the time scale: slow time (about a tenth of real time), fast time, step by step. [S4]
- The .NET garbage collector suspends managed threads during a collection; a sustained low latency mode exists for a long but bounded period. Measure the frame time in the real process. [S6]

## Sources

- [S1] Glenn Fiedler, "Fix Your Timestep!", 2004-06-10, <https://gafferongames.com/post/fix_your_timestep/>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S2] NASA, Trick documentation, "Executive Scheduler", <https://nasa.github.io/trick/documentation/simulation_capabilities/Executive-Scheduler>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S3] NASA, Trick documentation, "Realtime", <https://nasa.github.io/trick/documentation/simulation_capabilities/Realtime>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S4] IAEA, "Selection, specification, design and use of various nuclear power plant training simulators", IAEA-TECDOC-995, 1998, <https://www-pub.iaea.org/MTCD/publications/PDF/te_995_prn.pdf>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S5] ECSS, "Simulation modelling platform", ECSS-E-ST-40-07C Rev.1, 2025-08-05, <https://ecss.nl/wp-content/uploads/2025/08/ECSS-E-ST-40-07C-Rev.1(5August2025).pdf>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S6] Microsoft, "Latency modes" (garbage collection), updated 2021-09-15, <https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/standard/garbage-collection/latency>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.

# Training functions of a simulator

Contents: 1. Rules · 2. The functions · 3. The French word "reprise" · Sources

## 1. Rules for the code of training functions

- Every command of the instructor station goes through the executive, dated in simulated time.
- The whole state of a model is in serializable fields: nothing in a hidden variable. [S3] [S4]
- A malfunction or a forcing is data of the saved state, set by the instructor station, never a special case in the code. [S4]
- A model advances only when it is called: freeze needs nothing from it.

## 2. The functions (training simulator)

| Function | What it is | What the code must do |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Instructor station | The station from which the instructor commands the simulator; it can force inputs and outputs [S2] | Send commands to the executive, never to a model |
| Scenario | A list of operations, for example malfunctions, to train or examine [S2] | A list of events, dated or conditional |
| Initial conditions | A start state chosen by the instructor [S1] | Load a full state before the first step |
| Freeze, run | Simulated time stops while the program runs; Trick freezes at the end of a step or frame and keeps calling freeze jobs; ECSS runs only the entry points tied to the wall clock [S5] [S1] [S4] | Stop calling the models; keep the display alive |
| Snapshot, checkpoint | A saved state; Trick writes a readable text file, at a time, at the end or periodically; ECSS makes persistence mandatory for a model [S3] [S1] [S4] | Save and restore every field of every model |
| Backtrack | Automatic snapshots at a set interval, for example 30 to 600 s [S1] | A snapshot must be fast and never delay the frame |
| Replay | Replays from a backtrack point, without operator action [S1] | Replay recorded inputs on a deterministic simulator, or read recorded outputs |
| Malfunction | Triggered on demand, at a time or on an event, as a step or a ramp [S1] | A named fault value in the state of the model |
| Failure, forcing | ECSS: a persistent failure object; a model can declare itself failable; a field can be forcible [S4] | Failures and forcings are saved with the state |
| Remote functions | Local plant actions commanded from the instructor station [S1] | The same interface as operator actions |
| Slow and fast time | About a tenth of real time, or faster [S1] | A ratio set in the executive, never in a model |

## 3. "Reprise" means two things in French (freeze and restore)

"Reprise" names both the restart after a freeze and the restore of a saved state. In English and in code, keep two words: `Run` (or `Resume`) and `Restore`.

## Sources

- [S1] IAEA, "Selection, specification, design and use of various nuclear power plant training simulators", IAEA-TECDOC-995, 1998, <https://www-pub.iaea.org/MTCD/publications/PDF/te_995_prn.pdf>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S2] IAEA, "Guidelines for upgrade and modernization of nuclear power plant training simulators", IAEA-TECDOC-1500, 2006, <https://www-pub.iaea.org/MTCD/Publications/PDF/te_1500_web.pdf>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S3] NASA, Trick documentation, "Checkpoints", <https://nasa.github.io/trick/documentation/simulation_capabilities/Checkpoints>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S4] ECSS, "Simulation modelling platform", ECSS-E-ST-40-07C Rev.1, 2025-08-05, <https://ecss.nl/wp-content/uploads/2025/08/ECSS-E-ST-40-07C-Rev.1(5August2025).pdf>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S5] NASA, Trick documentation, "Executive Scheduler", <https://nasa.github.io/trick/documentation/simulation_capabilities/Executive-Scheduler>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.

# Simulation data to a web interface

Contents: 1. Three zones · 2. Documented means · 3. Rules for the gateway · Sources

## 1. Three zones (simulator and web interface)

Only zone 1 is held to the fixed step and to determinism; zones 2 and 3 are ordinary services.

```mermaid
flowchart TD
  subgraph Z1["Zone 1: stepped core, deterministic"]
    EXE["Fixed-step executive"] --> MOD["Models"]
  end
  subgraph Z2["Zone 2: services"]
    API["Instructor station, scenarios, snapshots"]
  end
  subgraph Z3["Zone 3: web streaming"]
    GW["Gateway: subscriptions, reduced rate"] --> UI["Browser: synoptics"]
  end
  MOD -->|"state published at each step"| GW
  API -->|"commands dated in simulated time"| EXE
  UI -->|"actions"| API
```

## 2. Documented means (push, history, rate)

| Need | Means | Source |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Push data from the server to the browser | SignalR: WebSockets first, then Server-Sent Events, then long polling; JSON or MessagePack (binary, smaller messages); advised for frequent updates, dashboards and monitoring | [S1] |
| Keep real time apart from history | Open MCT: a subscription delivers each new value; a request returns a past range, sorted in time | [S2] [S3] |
| Send no more than the screen shows | Open MCT: a request strategy that returns only the minimum and maximum per pixel | [S3] |
| Show at another rate than the computation | Interpolate between the two last states | [S4] |
| Signal limits | Open MCT: limit evaluators with five levels | [S3] |
| Link a web application to DIS or HLA | WebLVC, being standardized by SISO in 2016, through a message protocol and a gateway server; current status not checked | [S5] |

## 3. Rules for the gateway (synoptic streaming)

- The web interface reads published data; it never calls a model.
- Send the synoptics a state reduced to the screen rate (a few frames per second), not every computation step; on connection, a full state, then only the changes. Tune the rates by trial: no source gives a figure for a synoptic.
- Send operator actions to the services of zone 2, which date them in simulated time for the executive.
- With several gateway replicas, SignalR needs sticky sessions, or WebSockets only with negotiation skipped (`docs/architecture/` when present).

## Sources

- [S1] Microsoft, "Overview of ASP.NET Core SignalR", ASP.NET Core 10.0, <https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/aspnet/core/signalr/introduction>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S2] NASA, "Open MCT", README, <https://github.com/nasa/openmct>, read 2026-11-12, Apache 2.0, summarized.
- [S3] NASA, "Open MCT, API.md", <https://github.com/nasa/openmct/blob/master/API.md>, read 2026-11-12, Apache 2.0, summarized.
- [S4] Glenn Fiedler, "Fix Your Timestep!", 2004-06-10, <https://gafferongames.com/post/fix_your_timestep/>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S5] FFI, "Web Live, Virtual and Constructive (WebLVC)", report 16/01189, 2016, <https://www.ffi.no/en/publications-archive/web-live-virtual-and-constructive-weblvc>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.

# Sujet « synoptics » : représenter, nommer et modéliser les équipements

Ce dossier donne à l'IA les règles pour dessiner ou coder un symbole de synoptique, la convention de
nommage des propriétés, et un catalogue d'équipements : pour chacun, son symbole, son fonctionnement
réel, ses états, les noms de ses propriétés et un modèle simple de simulation.

| Fichier | Ce qu'il contient |
| --- | --- |
| `synoptic-rules.md` | La mise en page d'une vue, la couleur réservée à l'anormal, les états codés par un remplissage et un mot (jamais rouge et vert pour ouvert et fermé), les quatre priorités d'alerte, le clignotement à 1 Hz jusqu'à l'acquittement. |
| `symbol-property-naming.md` | La convention de nommage : camelCase, unité en suffixe tirée d'une liste fermée, booléens en `is`, `has` ou `can`, une énumération par groupe d'états, `commanded` et `actual`, `Setpoint`, les seuils d'alarme d'OPC UA, la position à quatre valeurs des organes de coupure. |
| `symbol-component.md` | Un symbole réutilisable comme fonction pure : entrées typées, attributs `data-*`, couleurs en variables CSS, une seule horloge de clignotement par vue. |
| `fluid-common.md` | Les rôles d'une propriété (paramètre, consigne, mesure, défaut, dérivée), les sept jeux de propriétés communs des équipements de fluides, et le calcul des états d'une vanne et d'une pompe. |
| `fluid-model-laws.md` | Les lois pour animer un synoptique de fluides (coefficient de débit, courbe de pompe, bilan de caisse, perte de charge) et les trois niveaux de détail d'un modèle. |
| `catalog-hydraulics.md` | 14 fiches d'hydraulique : pompes, moteur, vérin, distributeurs, clapets, limiteurs, accumulateur, filtre, réservoir, servovalve, refroidisseur. |
| `catalog-compressed-air.md` | 6 fiches d'air comprimé et les niveaux de pression d'un navire. |
| `catalog-piping-valves.md` | 8 fiches de robinetterie manuelle et automatique : vannes d'isolement, pointeau, clapets, soupapes, détendeur. |
| `catalog-actuated-valves.md` | 7 fiches : vannes motorisée, hydraulique, électrovanne, vanne de régulation, vanne trois voies, filtres, collecteur. |
| `catalog-ship-equipment.md` | 10 fiches d'équipements de bord : pompes, éjecteur, caisse, échangeur, réchauffeur, séparateurs, prise d'eau de mer, purgeur. |
| `catalog-ship-circuits.md` | 5 circuits d'un navire de surface (combustible, huile, refroidissement, assèchement, ballastage) et 4 fiches de sous-marin au niveau d'un manuel public. |
| `catalog-hvac.md` | 7 fiches de ventilation et de climatisation. |
| `index-fr-en-fluids.md` | Le nom anglais de chaque terme français des catalogues de fluides, avec le document qui le décrit. |
| `catalog-electrical-power.md` | 9 fiches électriques : groupe électrogène, alternateur, tableaux principal et de secours, disjoncteur, contacteur, sectionneur, transformateur, convertisseur. |
| `catalog-electrical-loads.md` | 6 fiches : batterie, chargeur, moteur et démarreur, prise de quai, délestage, couplage. |
| `catalog-fire-detection.md` | Les symboles des plans de lutte contre l'incendie (résolution OMI A.952(23)) et 6 fiches de détection : détecteurs, déclencheur manuel, sirène, centrale. |
| `catalog-fire-extinguishing.md` | 5 fiches : extinction au CO2, brouillard d'eau, mousse, sprinkleurs, porte coupe-feu ; le clapet coupe-feu renvoie à la ventilation. |
| `catalog-propulsion.md` | 11 fiches : moteur diesel, turbines, moteur de propulsion, ligne d'arbres, réducteur, hélices, appareil à gouverner, propulseur d'étrave, propulsion d'un sous-marin au niveau public. |
| `catalog-instrumentation.md` | Les lettres de repérage ISA 5.1 et 10 fiches : six transmetteurs, contacts, boucle 4-20 mA, seuils d'alarme, état de diagnostic. |
| `index-fr-en-systems.md` | Le nom anglais de chaque terme français des catalogues électrique, incendie, propulsion et instrumentation. |

Le gabarit TypeScript du symbole est `templates/typescript-angular/synoptic-symbol.ts`. Le contrôle de
la base refuse un nombre sans suffixe d'unité de la liste, un booléen qui ne commence pas par `is`,
`has` ou `can`, une fiche incomplète et une fiche absente de l'index français → anglais.

Manques connus : eau potable, vapeur (sauf le purgeur), ventilation et air d'un sous-marin ; la forme
exacte de chaque symbole reste à vérifier sur les normes achetées par l'équipe.

# Actuated valves and piping parts catalog

Contents: 1. Motor and hydraulic valves · 2. Solenoid and control valves · 3. Piping parts · Sources

Symbols: ASTM F1000, figure of valve accessories [S3], and ISA-5.1 for the actuator and instrument marks [S5]; cited, never copied. The valve body follows `catalog-piping-valves.md`. A motorized valve stays in place without power; a spring-return valve goes to its fail position: set `failPosition` on each valve. [S30]

## 1. Motor and hydraulic valves (actuated valves catalog)

### Motor-operated valve (vanne motorisée)

- Symbol: the valve symbol topped by a circle or a box; a letter marks the electric drive. [S3] [S5] [S50]
- Real working: a motor drives the stem through a self-locking worm gear, so the valve does not drift; limit switches give the end positions; torque switches protect a blocked valve; a transmitter can return the position; without power it stays in place; remote operation with a position indicator. [S30] [S15]
- States: `closed`, `opening`, `open`, `closing`, `stopped`, plus `hasPositionFault`.
- Properties: sets `onOffValve` and `controlMode`; `actualPositionPercent` (number, %, M, with a transmitter); `hasTorqueFault`, `hasPowerFault` (boolean, D).
- Model: open, close or stop order; the position moves by `100 / travelTimeS` % per second while the order lasts; limit switches at 100 % and 0 %; without power, the position freezes.

### Hydraulically operated valve (vanne à commande hydraulique)

- Symbol: the valve symbol topped by a piston actuator; a letter marks the hydraulic drive. [S3] [S50]
- Real working: fluid pressure pushes a piston that moves the valve; a fail-safe position can be set. Public example: the ballast vent valves of a 1946 submarine are hydraulically operated, with a hand back-up. [S30] [S16]
- States: `closed`, `opening`, `open`, `closing`, plus `hasPositionFault`.
- Properties: sets `onOffValve` and `controlMode`; `isHydraulicPressureAvailable` (boolean, M); `isHandOperated` (boolean, M).
- Model: a ramp as the motor-operated valve; no motion without hydraulic pressure, except by hand; on pressure loss, it goes to `failPosition`.

## 2. Solenoid and control valves (actuated valves catalog)

### Solenoid valve (électrovanne)

- Symbol: the valve symbol topped by a box with the coil letter. [S3] [S5]
- Real working: a coil pulls a plunger that opens or closes the passage; a spring returns it. Direct acting: simple, fast, power hungry. Pilot operated: the coil opens a small port and line pressure moves the diaphragm, which needs enough pressure difference. [S29]
- States: `deenergized`, `energized`, plus `hasCoilFault`.
- Properties: `isEnergized` (boolean, C); `isOpen` (boolean, X or M); `normalPosition` (enum `normallyClosed`, `normallyOpen`; P); `hasCoilFault` (boolean, D).
- Model: on or off, no travel time; back to `normalPosition` when power fails. [S29]

### Control valve and positioner (vanne de régulation et positionneur)

- Symbol: the valve symbol topped by a diaphragm actuator (half disk); the positioner is a small box next to the actuator. [S5] [S3]
- Real working: its opening follows the signal of a controller, acting on flow, so on pressure, temperature or level; the positioner compares the stem position with the command and corrects it despite friction and wear; a spring sends it open or closed when air fails; signals 4 to 20 mA or 3 to 15 psi. [S28]
- States: `closed`, `modulating`, `open`, `failSafe`, plus `hasPositionerFault`.
- Properties: sets `modulatingValve` and `controlMode`; `hasPositionerFault`, `hasAirSupplyFault` (boolean, D).
- Model: the position follows the command with a rise time [S34]; `Q = Kv × f(position) × √(Δp / relative density)` [S33]; on air loss, it goes to `failPosition`.

## 3. Piping parts (actuated valves catalog)

### Three-way valve (vanne trois voies)

- Symbol: the two-way valve with one more triangle: three triangles pointing to a common center. [S3] [S50]
- Real working: it splits or mixes two flows; the thermostatic valve of a lube oil circuit sets the temperature through the cooler [S22]; the three-way valve of an oily water separator sends off-spec water back to the tank [S18].
- States: `positionA`, `positionB`, `mixing`.
- Properties: `commandedPositionPercent`, `actualPositionPercent` (number, % toward port A, C and M); or `commandedPath`, `actualPath` (enum `a`, `b`) for an on-off version.
- Model: two coupled valves, opening `x` toward A and `1 − x` toward B; a thermostatic `x` follows the gap between measured and set temperature.

### Simplex and duplex strainer (filtre simple et filtre double)

- Symbol: a filter body with a dashed basket; two bodies and a change-over valve for the duplex. [S3]
- Real working: simplex, one element in a sump, cleaned by turning it against scrapers; duplex, two elements, one cleaned while the other stays in service. [S15]
- States: `clean`, `clogging`; duplex: element A or B in service.
- Properties: `differentialPressureBar` (number, bar, M); `hasCloggedAlarm` (boolean, D); `activeElement` (enum `a`, `b`; M, duplex).
- Model: `Δp = K(clogging) × Q²` on the element in service.

### Valve manifold (collecteur de vannes)

- Symbol: a row of valves on a common body. [S3]
- Real working: it takes suction from several sources and discharges to several destinations, for example to transfer fuel between tanks; suction valves are often stop-check valves, so that pumps do not lose their prime. [S15]
- States: those of each valve.
- Properties: one valve object per way (set `onOffValve`); `selectedSource`, `selectedDestination` (string, X).
- Model: one common node at one pressure; each way is an on-off valve.

## Sources

- [S3] ASTM International, "F1000-21 Standard Practice for Piping System Drawing Symbols", 2021, <https://store.astm.org/f1000-21.html>, read 2026-11-12, all rights reserved, cited by reference.
- [S5] International Society of Automation, "ANSI/ISA-5.1-2024, Instrumentation and Control, Symbols and Identification", <https://isa.org/standards-and-publications/isa-standards/isa-5-standard>, read 2026-11-12, all rights reserved, cited by reference.
- [S15] US Navy, "Pumps, Valves, and Piping", NAVEDTRA 14104A, chapter 9, <https://www.cedengineering.com/userfiles/Overview,%20Installation%20and%20Maintenance%20of%20Pumps,%20Valves%20and%20Piping.pdf>, read 2026-11-12, US government work, summarized.
- [S16] US Navy, Bureau of Naval Personnel, "The Fleet Type Submarine", 1946, chapter 4 "Tank Arrangements", <https://maritime.org/doc/fleetsub/chap4.php>, read 2026-11-12, US government work, summarized.
- [S18] Wikipedia, "Oily water separator (marine)", <https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oily_water_separator_(marine)>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY-SA, summarized.
- [S22] Wärtsilä, "Encyclopedia of Marine and Energy Technology, Main engine lubricating oil system", <https://www.wartsila.com/encyclopedia/term/main-engine-lubricating-oil-system>, read 2026-11-12, all rights reserved, summarized.
- [S28] Wikipedia, "Control valve", <https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Control_valve>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY-SA, summarized.

# Compressed air symbols catalog

Contents: 1. Pressure levels · 2. Production and storage · 3. Treatment · 4. Pressure and control · Sources

Symbol standards: ISO 1219-1 for pneumatic components [S1]; ISO 14617 for compressors [S2]; ASTM F1000 for ship piping, air flasks included [S3]. Cited, never copied. Roles and shared sets: `docs/synoptics/fluid-common.md`. A pneumatic cylinder uses the hydraulic cylinder card; the safety valve of an air receiver is in `catalog-piping-valves.md`; submarine air is in `catalog-ship-systems.md`.

## 1. Pressure levels (ship compressed air)

| Network | Nominal pressure | Use |
| --- | --- | --- |
| High pressure (HP) | 3,000 or 5,000 psi, about 207 or 345 bar | Above 1,000 psi; starting diesel engines and gas turbines |
| Medium pressure (MP) | 151 to 1,000 psi, about 10 to 69 bar | From an MP compressor or by reducing HP air |
| Low pressure (LP) | 150 psi and less, about 10 bar | The widest network on board |

Values of one navy, converted by the writer (1 psi ≈ 0.069 bar): a use, not a rule. Each project has its own. [S13]

## 2. Production and storage (compressed air catalog)

### Air compressor (compresseur d'air)

- Symbol: a circle with a triangle pointing outward, as a pump; an open triangle for air. [S1] [S2] [S49]
- Real working: it gives the air volume and pressure asked; a receiver in line smooths its pulses and lets it run unloaded when demand is low; coolers between stages and at the outlet remove some water. [S13] [S11]
- States: `stopped`, `loaded`, `unloaded`, plus `hasTripFault`.
- Properties: set `drive`; `isLoaded` (boolean, M); `dischargePressureBar` (number, bar, M); `dischargeTemperatureDegC` (number, °C, M); `cutInPressureBar`, `cutOutPressureBar` (number, bar, P).
- Model: constant flow when loaded, zero when unloaded; loads under `cutInPressureBar`, unloads above `cutOutPressureBar`.

### Air receiver and HP air flask (réservoir d'air et bouteille d'air haute pression)

- Symbol: ASTM F1000, figure of miscellaneous components, which covers flasks: a closed elongated shape. [S3]
- Real working: the receiver stores air for demand peaks; HP flasks add a high-pressure, high-flow reserve; receivers and flasks have low-point drains for water. [S13] [S11]
- States: `empty`, `low`, `normal`, `full`.
- Properties: `pressureBar` (number, bar, M); `volumeL` (number, L, P); `temperatureDegC` (number, °C, M); `hasLowPressureAlarm` (boolean, D); `isDrainOpen` (boolean, M).
- Model: ideal gas, `dp/dt = (mass inflow − mass outflow) × R × T / V`.

## 3. Treatment (compressed air catalog)

### Air dryer (sécheur d'air)

- Symbol: the diamond of conditioning devices; the navy list has a chemical dryer symbol. [S1] [S49] [S7]
- Real working: a refrigerated dryer passes air over a cold coil where water and oil condense; a desiccant dryer has two towers, one drying while the other regenerates with a reverse flow of hot dry air. Moisture corrodes, thins lubricants and freezes in cold weather. [S11] [S13]
- States: `stopped`, `drying`, `regenerating`, plus `hasDryerFault`.
- Properties: `isRunning` (boolean, M); `activeTower` (enum `a`, `b`; M, desiccant dryer); `outletDewPointDegC` (number, °C, M); `hasDryerFault` (boolean, D).
- Model: first-order delay of the outlet dew point toward a target; towers switch on a timer.

### Moisture separator and air filter (séparateur d'eau et filtre à air)

- Symbol: the diamond, with a mesh or a line for the element and a drain at the bottom. [S1] [S49]
- Real working: the separator removes liquid water; the filter stops solids (screens, porous elements, swirl chambers); low-point drains open at intervals. [S11] [S13]
- States: `clean`, `clogging`; drain `open` or `closed`.
- Properties: `differentialPressureBar` (number, bar, M); `hasCloggedAlarm` (boolean, D); `isDrainOpen` (boolean, M or C).
- Model: `Δp = K(clogging) × Q²`.

## 4. Pressure and control (compressed air catalog)

### Air pressure reducing station (poste de détente d'air)

- Symbol: ISO 1219-1 for the reducing valve (one box, spring, arrow in line, pilot from the outlet) [S1]; ASTM F1000 for its place in a piping diagram [S3].
- Real working: reducing stations lower HP air to a usable level; MP air can come from an HP flask bank through them; the valve holds a steady outlet pressure. [S13] [S9]
- States: `regulating`, `fullyOpen`, `closed`.
- Properties: `outletPressureSetpointBar` (number, bar, C); `inletPressureBar`, `outletPressureBar` (number, bar, M).
- Model: `outlet pressure = min(inlet pressure, setpoint)`.

### Pneumatic solenoid valve (électrovanne pneumatique)

- Symbol: boxes side by side with flow arrows, the coil at one end and the spring at the other; the navy list has an electric coil symbol. [S48] [S7]
- Real working: current in a coil pulls a plunger that opens or closes the passage; a spring returns it when the current stops; normally closed or normally open; direct or pilot-operated; a three-way valve links a port to supply or exhaust. [S29]
- States: `deenergized`, `energized`, plus `hasCoilFault`.
- Properties: `isEnergized` (boolean, C); `isOpen` (boolean, X or M); `normalPosition` (enum `normallyClosed`, `normallyOpen`; P); `hasCoilFault` (boolean, D).
- Model: on or off; response 5 to 150 ms by type, negligible on a synoptic; back to `normalPosition` when power fails. [S29]

## Sources

- [S1] ISO, "ISO 1219-1:2012 Fluid power systems and components, Graphical symbols and circuit diagrams, Part 1", catalog page, <https://www.boutique.afnor.org/en-gb/standard/iso-121912012/fluid-power-systems-and-components-graphic-symbols-and-circuit-diagrams-par/xs104720/120728>, read 2026-11-12, all rights reserved, cited by reference.
- [S2] Wikipedia, "ISO 14617", <https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ISO_14617>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY-SA, summarized.
- [S3] ASTM International, "F1000-21 Standard Practice for Piping System Drawing Symbols", 2021, <https://store.astm.org/f1000-21.html>, read 2026-11-12, all rights reserved, cited by reference.
- [S7] US Navy, "Fluid Power", NAVEDTRA 14105A, appendix "Graphic Symbols for Fluid Power Diagrams", <https://engineeringlibrary.org/reference/hydraulics-graphic-symbols-for-fluid-power-diagrams-navy>, read 2026-11-12, US government work, summarized.
- [S9] Same, chapter 6 "Valves", <https://engineeringlibrary.org/reference/hydraulic-valves-navy>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S11] Same, chapter 9, <https://engineeringlibrary.org/reference/hydraulic-reservoirs-strainers-filters-accumulators-navy>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S13] Same, chapter 11 "Pneumatics", <https://engineeringlibrary.org/reference/pneumatics-navy>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S29] Wikipedia, "Solenoid valve", <https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Solenoid_valve>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY-SA, summarized.
- [S48] STAUFF, "Correctly reading circuit symbols according to DIN ISO 1219", 2025-08-19, <https://stauff.co.uk/en/blog/products/correctly-reading-circuit-symbols-according-to-din-iso-1219>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S49] Carr Lane Manufacturing, "Hydraulic Symbols and How to Read Them", <https://www.carrlane.com/engineering-resources/technical-information/power-workholding/design-information/hydraulic-symbols-and-diagrams>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.

# Electrical storage, loads and power functions catalog

Contents: 1. Storage · 2. Loads and shore · 3. Power functions · Sources

Symbols come from IEC 60617, a paid database: "usual shape" is an analysis to check on the standard. Generation, switchboards and switching devices are in `catalog-electrical-power.md`. Every card also takes the common inputs of `symbol-property-naming.md`.

## 1. Storage (electrical catalog)

### Storage battery (batterie d'accumulateurs)

- Symbol: IEC 60617; usual shape, alternate long and short lines; fire plan symbol IMO 2.35. [S1]
- Real working: the state of charge is the usable capacity left, 0 to 100 %; it is not measured directly but estimated from the voltage or by integrating the current, which drifts and is reset when the charger sees full charge. [S22]
- States: `charging`, `discharging`, `floating`, `depleted`.
- Properties: `voltageV` (number, V, M); `currentA` (number, A, M, positive when discharging); `stateOfChargePercent` (number, %, M); `capacityAh` (number, Ah, P); `temperatureDegC` (number, °C, M); `isCharging` (boolean, M); `timeRemainingS` (number, s, M); `hasLowVoltageAlarm` (boolean, D).
- Model: `SoC = SoC0 − (1 / capacity) × ∫ I dt`; voltage = function of the state of charge minus the inner drop `R × I`. Public names: Signal K `electrical.batteries.*`, IEC 61850 ZBAT. [S2] [S6]

### Battery charger (chargeur de batterie)

- Symbol: IEC 60617; usual shape, the rectifier symbol linked to the battery.
- Real working: a rectifier that turns AC into DC to charge the battery [S62]; a public data model gives it a charging mode, a voltage setpoint and a current setpoint [S2].
- States: `off`, `bulk`, `absorption`, `float`, plus `hasChargerFault`.
- Properties: `isOn` (boolean, M); `chargingMode` (enum, M); `outputVoltageV`, `voltageSetpointV` (number, V, M and C); `outputCurrentA`, `currentLimitA` (number, A, M and C); `hasSupplyFault`, `hasChargerFault` (boolean, D).
- Model: constant current up to the voltage setpoint, then constant voltage.

## 2. Loads and shore (electrical catalog)

### Motor and starter (moteur et démarreur)

- Symbol: IEC 60617; usual shape, a circle marked "M"; the starter is a rectangle or the contactor with its thermal relay.
- Real working: an induction motor turns slightly slower than its field, a slip of about 0.5 to 5 %; 4 poles at 50 Hz give a 1,500 rpm field; direct-on-line start draws 6 to 7 times rated current, reduced by star-delta, autotransformer, soft starter or drive [S19]; the starter joins a contactor and protections (overload, undervoltage) [S14].
- States: `stopped`, `starting`, `running`, plus `isOverloadTripped`.
- Properties: `isStartCommanded`, `isStopCommanded` (boolean, C); `isRunning`, `isOverloadTripped` (boolean, M); `speedRpm` (number, rpm, M); `currentA` (number, A, M); `ratedCurrentA` (number, A, P); `startingMethod` (enum `directOnLine`, `starDelta`, `autotransformer`, `softStarter`, `variableFrequencyDrive`; P); `startCount` (integer, M); `runTimeH` (number, h, M).
- Model: current 6 to 7 × rated during the start time, then proportional to load; speed first-order to `(1 − slip) × field speed`; trips on a lasting overload. Public name: IEC 61850 ZMOT. [S6]

### Shore connection (prise de quai)

- Symbol: IEC 60617; usual shape, a socket and plug with their breaker.
- Real working: shore power feeds the ship at berth with engines stopped, through a suitable cable; large installations convert the voltage and frequency of the grid; IEC/ISO/IEEE 80005-1 covers ships over 1 MVA. [S20]
- States: `disconnected`, `connected`, `supplying`.
- Properties: `isCableConnected`, `isShoreSupplyAvailable`, `isShoreBreakerClosed`, `isPhaseSequenceCorrect` (boolean, M); `shoreVoltageV` (number, V, M); `shoreFrequencyHz` (number, Hz, M); `shorePowerKw` (number, kW, M).
- Model: an external source of unlimited power; interlocked with the ship generators (no lasting parallel run, to confirm on the simulated ship).
- Pitfall: on a fire plan, "international shore connection" (IMO 2.14) is the fire main coupling, "raccord international de jonction avec la terre", not the electrical shore connection. [S1] [S68]

## 3. Power functions (electrical catalog)

### Load shedding (délestage)

- Symbol: a function, with no symbol of its own; on a synoptic, a mark on the non-essential feeders.
- Real working: preferential trips disconnect non-essential consumers on overload, to avoid the main breaker trip and a blackout; loads go in steps at 5, 10 and 15 seconds (air conditioning, ventilation, galley); the power management system sheds load in two steps when consumption exceeds production. [S12] [S11]
- States: `inactive`, `stage1`, `stage2`, `stage3`.
- Properties: `isLoadSheddingActive` (boolean, M); `sheddingStage` (integer, M); `overloadThresholdPercent` (number, %, P); `stageDelayS` (number, s, P); `sheddingGroup` (integer, P); `isNonEssential` (boolean, P); `isResetCommanded` (boolean, C).
- Model: when the load of a set stays above the threshold for the delay of a step, open the feeders of that shedding group.

### Synchronizing (couplage)

- Symbol: a function; usual shape on a synoptic, a synchronism indicator near the generator breaker.
- Real working: before closing a set on a live busbar, voltage, frequency, phase sequence and phase angle must match; a synchroscope shows whether the set runs fast or slow; a synchro-check relay blocks closing outside the window; an automatic synchronizer does it alone [S16]; closing out of phase gives a high current and can damage the set [S21].
- States: `idle`, `synchronizing`, `synchronized`, plus `hasSyncFailure`.
- Properties: `isSyncCommanded` (boolean, C); `isAutoSyncActive`, `isSynchronized` (boolean, M); `voltageDifferenceV` (number, V, M); `frequencyDifferenceHz` (number, Hz, M); `phaseAngleDeg` (number, °, M); `hasSyncFailure` (boolean, D).
- Model: the phase angle integrates the frequency gap, `dφ/dt = 360 × Δf`; closing allowed when the voltage, frequency and angle gaps are inside their windows.

## Sources

- [S1] International Maritime Organization, "Resolution A.952(23), Graphical symbols for shipboard fire control plans", 2004, <https://wwwcdn.imo.org/localresources/en/OurWork/Safety/Documents/A.23_Res.952.pdf>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S2] Signal K, "Signal K Specification 1.7.0, Appendix A: Keys Reference (Vessel)", <https://signalk.org/specification/1.7.0/doc/vesselsBranch.html>, read 2026-11-12, Apache 2.0, summarized.
- [S6] IEC, "IEC 61850-7-4 edition 2.0", bilingual table of contents, <https://assets.vde-verlag.de/iec-normen/preview-pdf/info_iec61850-7-4%7Bed2.0%7Db.pdf>, read 2026-11-12, all rights reserved, cited by reference.
- [S11] Wikipedia, "Power management system", <https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Power_management_system>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY-SA, summarized.
- [S12] Marine Insight, "Preferential Trips on Ship: Construction and Working", <https://www.marineinsight.com/marine-safety/preferential-trips-on-ship-construction-and-working/>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S14] Wikipedia, "Contactor", <https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Contactor>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY-SA, summarized.
- [S16] Wikipedia, "Synchronization (alternating current)", <https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Synchronization_(alternating_current)>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY-SA, summarized.
- [S19] Wikipedia, "Induction motor", <https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Induction_motor>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY-SA, summarized.
- [S20] Wikipedia, "Shore power", <https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shore_power>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY-SA, summarized.
- [S21] Wikipedia, "Diesel generator", <https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diesel_generator>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY-SA, summarized.
- [S22] Wikipedia, "State of charge", <https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_of_charge>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY-SA, summarized.
- [S62] Wikipedia, "Power electronics", <https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Power_electronics>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY-SA, summarized.

sources: [S1, S2, S3, S6, S10, S11, S13, S14, S15, S17, S18, S21, S25, S61, S62, S63, S66, S67, S68, S69]

# Electrical generation and distribution catalog

Contents: 1. Symbols · 2. Generation · 3. Switchboards · 4. Switching devices · 5. Transformer and converter · Sources

## 1. Symbols of the electrical catalog

- Electrical symbols come from IEC 60617, a paid database: no symbol was read for this base. "Usual shape" is an analysis: check it on the standard before drawing.
- In IEC practice a circuit breaker is a switching contact with one or more qualifier signs; the exact shape depends on the diagram type and the legend of the project. The old IEEE/ANSI 315-1975 is inactive but still in old drawings. [S69]
- On a fire control plan, the emergency generator, emergency battery and emergency switchboard have IMO symbols 2.34 to 2.36. [S1]
- Every card also takes the common inputs of `symbol-property-naming.md` (`health`, `controlLocation`, alarm priority and state). Switching devices use `switchPosition` with four values, as in IEC 61850. [S10]

## 2. Generation (electrical catalog)

### Generator set (groupe électrogène)

- Symbol: IEC 60617; usual shape, a circle marked "G" linked to its prime mover; fire plan symbol IMO 2.34. [S1]
- Real working: a diesel engine drives an alternator; a governor holds speed, so frequency; with 4 to 5 % droop, power follows the gap between speed and setpoint, and sets with the same droop share load by their rating; the power management system starts a second set when the first reaches 80 % of its capacity. [S21] [S17] [S11]
- States: `stopped`, `starting`, `running`, `connected`, `stopping`, plus `hasTripFault`.
- Properties: `isStartCommanded`, `isStopCommanded` (boolean, C); `isRunning` (boolean, M); `speedRpm`, `speedSetpointRpm` (number, rpm, M and C); `activePowerKw` (number, kW, M); `loadPercent` (number, %, M); `droopPercent` (number, %, P); `runTimeH` (number, h, M); `hasStartFault`, `hasOverspeedFault` (boolean, D); `startPriority` (integer, C).
- Model: start is a delay; speed is a first-order move to the setpoint; `P = Pn × (f0 − f) / (droop × fn)`; motion `J dω/dt = Tm − Te`. [S18] Public names: Signal K `propulsion.*`, IEC 61850 ZGEN. [S2] [S6]

### Alternator (alternateur)

- Symbol: IEC 60617; usual shape, a circle marked "G" with the alternating current sign.
- Real working: a rotating field induces an alternating voltage in the stator; frequency depends on speed and poles (2 poles at 3,000 rpm give 50 Hz); the automatic voltage regulator sets the excitation to hold voltage and to share reactive power. [S63]
- States: `deExcited`, `excited`, `regulating`, plus `hasAvrFault`.
- Properties: `voltageV`, `voltageSetpointV` (number, V, M and C); `currentA`, `excitationCurrentA` (number, A, M); `frequencyHz` (number, Hz, M); `activePowerKw` (number, kW, M); `reactivePowerKvar` (number, kvar, M); `powerFactorRatio` (number, 0 to 1, M); `windingTemperatureDegC` (number, °C, M); `hasAvrFault` (boolean, D).
- Model: voltage is a first-order move to the setpoint, with a dip at a load step; frequency = poles × rpm / 120. Public names: Signal K `electrical.ac.*`, IEC 61850 MMXU. [S2] [S6]

## 3. Switchboards (electrical catalog)

### Main switchboard (tableau principal)

- Symbol: IEC 60617; on a single-line diagram, a thick busbar line with the feeders on it.
- Real working: it takes the main generator sets and distributes power; rules ask for two main sets at least and a switchboard placed so that a casualty hits only its own space; the power management system starts and stops sets, synchronizes, shares load, sheds load, restores after a blackout and blocks large consumers. [S25] [S11]
- States: `dead`, `energized`, `overloaded`.
- Properties: `isEnergized` (boolean, M); `busVoltageV` (number, V, M); `busFrequencyHz` (number, Hz, M); `totalLoadKw`, `availablePowerKw` (number, kW, M); `isBusTieClosed` (boolean, M); `hasEarthFault`, `hasBlackout` (boolean, D).
- Model: power produced = power consumed; no closed source on the busbar, zero voltage (blackout).

### Emergency switchboard and emergency source (tableau de secours et source de secours)

- Symbol: fire plan symbols IMO 2.34 (generator), 2.35 (battery), 2.36 (switchboard) [S1]; IEC 60617 on the electrical diagram.
- Real working: the emergency source is independent, above the bulkhead deck, outside machinery spaces: a battery or a set with its own fuel, starting automatically on new ships; a transitional battery holds half an hour for emergency lighting, watertight doors and alarms [S25]. One secondary course gives 45 seconds at most to take the required services (single source). [S66]
- States: `fedFromMain`, `blackout`, `emergencyStarting`, `fedFromEmergency`.
- Properties: `supplyState` (enum, M); `isFedFromMain`, `isFedFromEmergency`, `isInterconnectorClosed`, `isTransitionalSourceActive` (boolean, M); `emergencyStartDelayS` (number, s, P); `busVoltageV` (number, V, M); `hasEmergencyStartFault` (boolean, D).
- Model: loss of main supply, start delay, emergency breaker closes, emergency services fed. French official terms: [S68].

## 4. Switching devices (electrical catalog)

### Circuit breaker (disjoncteur)

- Symbol: IEC 60617, a switching contact with a qualifier sign [S69]; usual shape, an oblique open contact with a cross at its end.
- Real working: it cuts the current on overcurrent and can be reset, unlike a fuse; a bimetal reacts to long overloads, a solenoid to high currents; a spring or air parts the contacts and the arc is quenched in air, oil, SF6 or vacuum; after a fault trip it must be reset before closing. [S13]
- States: `switchPosition` `intermediate`, `open`, `closed`, `bad` (bad only when the auxiliary contacts disagree), plus `isTripped`. [S10]
- Properties: `switchPosition` (enum, M); `isClosed`, `isTripped`, `isCloseBlocked` (boolean, M); `isCloseCommanded`, `isOpenCommanded`, `isResetCommanded` (boolean, C); `tripCause` (enum `overload`, `shortCircuit`, `undervoltage`, `external`; M); `currentA` (number, A, M); `ratedCurrentA` (number, A, P); `isNormallyOpen` (boolean, P); `operationCount` (integer, M).
- Model: ideal switch with an operating delay; trips when the current passes a threshold for a time; stays tripped until reset. Public names: IEC 61850 XCBR and CSWI, CIM `Switch.normalOpen`. [S6] [S10] [S67]

### Contactor (contacteur)

- Symbol: IEC 60617; usual shape, a contact with a half circle and a rectangular coil.
- Real working: a switch driven by a coil, for heavy currents (motors, lighting, heating), not made to cut a short circuit; the coil pulls a core that closes the contacts, a spring opens them; with an overload relay it makes a motor starter. [S14]
- States: `open`, `closed`, plus `hasWeldedContactFault`.
- Properties: `isClosed`, `isCoilEnergized`, `isAuxiliaryContactClosed` (boolean, M); `isCloseCommanded` (boolean, C); `hasWeldedContactFault` (boolean, D); `operationCount` (integer, M).
- Model: follows the command with a short delay; opens when the control voltage disappears.

### Disconnector (sectionneur)

- Symbol: IEC 60617; usual shape, a contact with a short perpendicular line at its end.
- Real working: it isolates a circuit visibly for maintenance; it cannot quench an arc and opens only after another device has cut the current; often paired with an earthing switch, with interlocks on the order of operations. [S15]
- States: `switchPosition` `open`, `closed`, `intermediate`, `bad`, plus `isEarthed`.
- Properties: `switchPosition` (enum, M); `isClosed`, `isEarthed`, `isLocked`, `isOperationAllowed` (boolean, M); `isCloseCommanded`, `isOpenCommanded` (boolean, C); `hasInterlockViolation` (boolean, D).
- Model: a slow ideal switch; the operation is refused, or raises a fault, while the linked breaker is closed. Public names: IEC 61850 XSWI and CILO. [S6]

## 5. Transformer and converter (electrical catalog)

### Power transformer (transformateur de puissance)

- Symbol: IEC 60617; usual shape, two overlapping circles or two windings face to face.
- Real working: a varying current in one winding makes a flux in the core that induces a voltage in the other; the voltage ratio equals the turns ratio; copper and iron losses, a high inrush current at energizing, galvanic isolation. [S61]
- States: `deEnergized`, `energized`, `overloaded`.
- Properties: `isEnergized` (boolean, M); `primaryVoltageV`, `secondaryVoltageV` (number, V, M); `turnsRatio` (number, P); `loadPercent` (number, %, M); `windingTemperatureDegC` (number, °C, M); `tapPosition` (integer, M); `hasOvertemperatureFault` (boolean, D).
- Model: `V2 = V1 × N2 / N1`; constant efficiency; inrush ignored or a short peak. Public names: IEC 61850 YPTR, YLTC. [S6]

### Power converter (convertisseur)

- Symbol: IEC 60617; usual shape, a square crossed by a diagonal, with the input sign on one side and the output sign on the other.
- Real working: rectifier (AC to DC), inverter (DC to AC), chopper (DC to DC), AC to AC converter changing voltage or frequency; a drive sets the speed of an induction motor; switching injects harmonics to filter. [S62]
- States: `off`, `starting`, `running`, `currentLimited`, plus `hasConverterFault`.
- Properties: `converterType` (enum `rectifier`, `inverter`, `dcDc`, `frequencyConverter`; P); `isStartCommanded`, `isStopCommanded` (boolean, C); `isRunning` (boolean, M); `inputVoltageV`, `outputVoltageV` (number, V, M); `outputFrequencyHz`, `outputFrequencySetpointHz` (number, Hz, M and C); `outputCurrentA` (number, A, M); `loadPercent` (number, %, M); `hasConverterFault` (boolean, D).
- Model: output = setpoint through a ramp; constant efficiency; current limit. Public names: IEC 61850 ZCON, Signal K `electrical.inverters.*`. [S6] [S2]

# Fire control plan symbols and fire detection catalog

Contents: 1. Fire control plan symbols · 2. Detectors · 3. Call point and alarm · Sources

The encyclopedia pages cited describe building equipment; ship rules come from IMO and flag texts [S1] [S37] [S38]. Extinguishing systems, fire doors and fire dampers are in `catalog-fire-extinguishing.md` and `catalog-hvac.md`.

## 1. Fire control plan symbols (IMO A.952(23))

The reference for a ship fire control plan is IMO resolution A.952(23), adopted on 2003-12-05, not ISO 7010. [S1]

| Group | Numbers | Content |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Structural fire protection | 1.1 to 1.18 | Class A and B divisions, main vertical zone, fire doors (hinged or sliding, self-closing or not), remote controls, fire damper, ventilation closures |
| Fire-protection appliances | 2.1 to 2.44 | Fire pumps and controls, remote stops of fuel and oil pumps, remote release station, international shore connection, hydrants, section valves, fixed systems, extinguishers, emergency sources and switchboard, detection panel, general alarm, call point, spaces watched by smoke, heat, flame or gas detectors |
| Means of escape | 3.1 to 3.3 | Primary and secondary escape routes, emergency escape breathing device |

- Ventilation codes (1.12, 1.15 to 1.18): circle colour and letter, blue "A" accommodation and service spaces, green "M" machinery spaces, yellow "C" cargo spaces. [S1]
- Extinguishing agent codes: grey CO2 or nitrogen, brown "H" other gases, white "P" powder, green "W" water, yellow "F" foam, orange (2.32) sprinklers or high-pressure water. [S1]
- Every plan carries a legend of the symbols it uses; ships built before 2004-01-01 may keep the symbols of resolution A.654(16). [S1]
- ISO 17631:2002, cited by the resolution, was withdrawn on 2022-12-07 and replaced by ISO 17631:2022, which adds damage control. [S28]
- ISO 7010 signs (red square, F001 to F019) are the signs fixed on board, another family. [S26]
- EN 54 parts: 2 control panel, 3 sounders, 5 heat, 7 smoke, 10 flame detectors, 11 call points, 23 visual alarm devices. [S27]

## 2. Detectors (fire detection catalog)

Each detector also has `loopId`, `deviceAddress` and `zoneId` (string, P) when it is addressable.

### Smoke detector (détecteur de fumée)

- Symbol: IMO 2.41, space watched by smoke detectors [S1]; component EN 54-7 [S27].
- Real working: optical, a light source and a photodiode in a chamber, smoke scatters light to the sensor; ionization, smoke lowers the current of an ionized chamber against a reference; an addressable detector has its own number, so the panel knows which one is in alarm and can report contamination; dust and cooking fumes cause false alarms [S29]. On board, smoke detectors are in stairways, corridors and escape routes of accommodation spaces [S37].
- States: `normal`, `alarm`, `disabled`, plus `hasFault`.
- Properties: `isAlarm`, `isContaminated` (boolean, M); `hasFault` (boolean, D); `isDisabled` (boolean, C); `smokeLevelPercent` (number, %, M); `alarmThresholdPercent` (number, %, P).
- Model: smoke level of the space, first-order delay, threshold, alarm latched until the panel reset. Public name: IFC `SMOKESENSOR`. [S59]

### Heat detector (détecteur de chaleur)

- Symbol: IMO 2.42 [S1]; component EN 54-5 [S27].
- Real working: fixed temperature, an alloy melts at its rating, often 58 °C in buildings; rate-of-rise, it reacts to a rise of about 6.7 to 8.3 °C per minute whatever the start; every heat detector lags the room air. [S30]
- States: `normal`, `alarm`, `disabled`, plus `hasFault`.
- Properties: `isAlarm` (boolean, M); `hasFault` (boolean, D); `isDisabled` (boolean, C); `temperatureDegC` (number, °C, M); `fixedThresholdDegC` (number, °C, P); `rateOfRiseThresholdDegCPerMin` (number, °C/min, P); `responseTimeS` (number, s, P).
- Model: element temperature, first-order delay of the room temperature; alarm above the threshold or when the rise passes the rate threshold. Public name: IFC `HEATSENSOR`. [S59]

### Flame detector (détecteur de flamme)

- Symbol: IMO 2.43 [S1]; component EN 54-10 [S27].
- Real working: it senses flame radiation, ultraviolet, infrared or both against false alarms; ultraviolet reacts in milliseconds but a 2 to 3 second delay is often added; infrared answers in 3 to 5 seconds; arc welding, sun and hot surfaces fool it; water or oil on the lens blinds it. [S31]
- States: `normal`, `alarm`, `disabled`, plus `hasFault`.
- Properties: `isAlarm`, `isLensObscured` (boolean, M); `hasFault` (boolean, D); `isDisabled` (boolean, C); `detectorType` (enum `uv`, `ir`, `uvIr`, `multiIr`; P); `responseDelayS` (number, s, P).
- Model: flame in the field of view (boolean), response delay, alarm latched. Public name: IFC `FIRESENSOR`. [S59]

## 3. Call point and alarm (fire detection catalog)

### Manual call point (déclencheur manuel)

- Symbol: IMO 2.40 [S1]; sign ISO 7010 F005 [S26]; component EN 54-11 [S27].
- Real working: a person presses a button or breaks a glass to raise the alarm; a light shows the point used; it is reset by hand with a key [S32]. On board: in accommodation, service spaces and control stations, one at each exit, no point of a corridor more than 20 m from one [S37].
- States: `normal`, `activated`, `disabled`, plus `hasFault`.
- Properties: `isActivated` (boolean, M); `hasFault` (boolean, D); `isDisabled`, `isLocalResetCommanded` (boolean, C).
- Model: a latched boolean until the local reset, then the panel reset. Public names: IFC `BREAKGLASSBUTTON`, `MANUALPULLBOX`. [S59]

### Sounder and beacon (sirène et flash)

- Symbol: sign ISO 7010 F018 [S26]; components EN 54-3 and EN 54-23 [S27]; no IMO symbol of its own, next to 2.39 general alarm [S1].
- Real working: audible (bells, horns, electronic sirens), visible (strobes) or voice devices; the panel sets the tone pattern [S39]. On board, a sounder outside the detection system needs two supplies, one of them emergency [S38].
- States: `off`, `sounding`, `silenced`, plus `hasFault`.
- Properties: `isSoundCommanded` (boolean, C); `isSounding`, `isFlashing`, `isSilenced` (boolean, M); `tonePattern` (enum, P); `hasFault` (boolean, D).
- Model: follows the panel output; silencing stops the sound, not the alarm.

### Fire detection control panel (centrale de détection incendie)

- Symbol: IMO 2.38 [S1]; component EN 54-2 [S27].
- Real working: it takes the detector signals, checks that they work and drives the linked equipment by a set sequence; conventional, it knows the zone; addressable, each device on its loops [S33]. On board it shows doors, detectors, alarms and fans; an open detector circuit gives an alarm; a detector can stop fans and close fire doors and dampers [S37].
- States: `normal`, `preAlarm`, `alarm`, `alarmSilenced`, `fault`. [S33]
- Properties: `panelState` (enum, M); `activeAlarmCount`, `activeFaultCount`, `disabledDeviceCount` (integer, M); `firstAlarmZoneId` (string, M); `isAcknowledgeCommanded`, `isSilenceCommanded`, `isResetCommanded` (boolean, C); `isOnBackupPower`, `isVentilationStopOutputActive`, `isDoorReleaseOutputActive` (boolean, M); `hasPowerFault`, `hasLoopFault` (boolean, D).
- Model: a state machine over the devices; commands acknowledge, silence, reset; outputs sounders, door release, damper closing, ventilation stop. French official term: [S68].

- [S26] Wikipedia, "ISO 7010", <https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ISO_7010>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY-SA, summarized.

- [S30] Wikipedia, "Heat detector", <https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heat_detector>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY-SA, summarized.
- [S31] Wikipedia, "Flame detector", <https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flame_detector>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY-SA, summarized.
- [S32] Wikipedia, "Manual fire alarm activation", <https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manual_fire_alarm_activation>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY-SA, summarized.

- [S38] International Maritime Organization, "MSC.1/Circ.1487", 2014, <https://www.mardep.gov.hk/en/msnote/pdf/msin1520anx1.pdf>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S39] Wikipedia, "Fire alarm notification appliance", <https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fire_alarm_notification_appliance>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY-SA, summarized.

- [S68] French Ministry for the Sea, "Division 221", edition of 2023-07-26, <https://mer.gouv.fr/sites/default/files/2023-08/d221_%2826-07-23%29.pdf>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
