# Fluid model laws and levels of detail

Contents: 1. Laws · 2. Levels of detail · 3. Keep it simple · Sources

## 1. Four laws and two relations (fluid model)

| Law | Form | Use | Limits |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Valve flow coefficient | `Q = Kv × √(Δp / (ρ / ρ0))`, `Q` in m³/h, `Δp` in bar, `ρ0` density of water. `Kv`: water flow in m³/h under 1 bar | Any valve or orifice [S33] [S38] | Liquids. For a gas, only while the pressure drop stays under 0.2 times the inlet absolute pressure; beyond, choked flow models. Not for non-Newtonian fluids or slurries [S34] [S42] |
| Pump curve and affinity | Head-flow curve at rated speed, linear, quadratic or polynomial; at another speed, flow scales with speed, head with its square, power with its cube | Centrifugal pumps, fans [S35] [S36] [S40] | Similar machines, efficiency taken constant. A positive displacement pump gives a flow proportional to speed, whatever the pressure [S10] |
| Tank volume balance | `dV/dt = inflows − outflows`; `level = V / area`; bottom pressure = height × specific weight | Tanks, sumps, ballast tanks [S14] [S37] | Constant cross-section; stop the computation when the tank overflows |
| Pressure loss | `Δp = f × (L / D) × ρ × v² / 2`; turbulent: loss grows as the square of the speed; laminar: `f = 64 / Re` | Pipes, filters, exchangers [S41] | For a synoptic: `Δp = K × Q²`, one coefficient per branch |
| Cylinder force | `F = p × A`; rod side: piston area − rod area | Cylinders [S14] | Friction ignored |
| Speed and flow | `Q = v × A` | Cylinders, pipes [S14] | Incompressible fluid |

- `Cv` is the water flow in US gallons per minute under 1 psi at 60 °F. Use one factor in the whole project: `Cv = 1.16 × Kv`. [S39]
- Control valve sizing follows IEC 60534-2-1; its liquid equations assume an incompressible Newtonian fluid. [S42]
- Opening dynamics: filter the opening order with a rise time, or move on a ramp, to avoid unrealistic flow reversals. [S34]
- Gas volumes (air receiver, accumulator): ideal gas law and gas compression (elementary physics, analysis).

## 2. Three levels of model detail (fluid synoptic)

| Level | What it computes | Fits |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 0. Logic | States only. A line is "fed" when a path of open valves links it to a running source | Colouring pipes, checking a valve line-up, procedure training [S34] |
| 1. Quasi-static | One flow and one pressure per branch: valve law, pump curve, `Δp = K × Q²`; levels integrated in time | Credible flows, pressures and levels; alarms and trips [S33] [S35] [S41] |
| 2. Dynamic | Level 1 plus opening delays, temperatures, gases, start-ups | Control loops, transients, fine faults [S34] |

Choose level 1 by default, level 0 for circuits without a shown measure, level 2 only for the part a scenario plays.

## 3. Keep the model simple (fluid synoptic)

- One coefficient per branch.
- No implicit equation when an explicit rule is enough: `outlet pressure = min(inlet pressure, setpoint)`.
- Every fault parameter is a named property: `cloggingPercent`, `failPosition`.
- The simplest public valve model, with a pressure loss proportional to flow, is presented by its documentation as a test model: say so when you use it. [S34]

## Sources

- [S10] US Navy, "Fluid Power", NAVEDTRA 14105A, chapter 4 "Pumps", <https://engineeringlibrary.org/reference/hydraulic-pumps-navy>, read 2026-11-12, US government work, summarized.
- [S14] Same, chapter 2 "Forces in Liquids", <https://engineeringlibrary.org/reference/hydraulics-forces-in-liquids-navy>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S33] Modelica Association, "Modelica.Fluid User's Guide, Valve characteristics", <https://doc.modelica.org/om/Modelica.Fluid.UsersGuide.ComponentDefinition.ValveCharacteristics.html>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S34] Modelica Association, "Modelica.Fluid.Valves", Modelica Standard Library 4.0.0, <https://doc.modelica.org/Modelica%204.0.0/Resources/helpDymola/Modelica_Fluid_Valves.html>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S35] Modelica Association, "Modelica.Fluid.Machines.BaseClasses.PartialPump", <https://doc.modelica.org/om/Modelica.Fluid.Machines.BaseClasses.PartialPump.html>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S36] Modelica Association, "PumpCharacteristics", <https://doc.modelica.org/om/Modelica.Fluid.Machines.BaseClasses.PumpCharacteristics.html>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S37] Modelica Association, "Modelica.Fluid.Vessels.OpenTank", <https://doc.modelica.org/om/Modelica.Fluid.Vessels.OpenTank.html>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.

- [S40] Wikipedia, "Affinity laws", <https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Affinity_laws>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY-SA, summarized.
- [S41] Wikipedia, "Darcy–Weisbach equation", <https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Darcy%E2%80%93Weisbach_equation>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY-SA, summarized.

# synoptics index

One line per document. Open only the documents your task needs.

# Symbol component

Contents: 1. Structure · 2. Inputs and view function · 3. Markup · 4. Styles · 5. Flash clock · 6. Pitfalls · 7. Final check · Sources

## 1. Structure (symbol component)

- One symbol is one pure function: the same inputs always give the same picture.
- The symbol reads no data by itself. It starts no timer. It keeps no state.
- The inputs carry states and values. They never carry a colour.
- The function turns the inputs into `data-*` attributes, one state word and one accessible name.
- The styles choose the fill, the pattern and the badge from the `data-*` attributes.
- Every colour comes from a CSS variable set on the view.
- Follow the document "Symbol property naming" for every input name.

## 2. Inputs and view function (symbol component)

```ts
export type PositionState = 'open' | 'closed' | 'opening' | 'closing' | 'intermediate' | 'unknown';
export type AlarmPriority = 'none' | 'caution' | 'warning' | 'alarm' | 'emergency';
export type AlarmState = 'normal' | 'unacknowledged' | 'acknowledged';

export interface ValveSymbolInputs {
  readonly tagName: string;
  readonly positionState: PositionState;
  readonly actualPositionPercent?: number;
  readonly alarmPriority: AlarmPriority;
  readonly alarmState: AlarmState;
  readonly isOutOfService: boolean;
}

export interface SymbolView {
  readonly attributes: Readonly<Record<string, string>>;
  readonly stateText: string;
  readonly badgeLetter: string;
  readonly isBadgeFlashing: boolean;
  readonly accessibleName: string;
}

const POSITION_TEXT: Readonly<Record<PositionState, string>> = {
  open: 'OPEN',
  closed: 'CLOSED',
  opening: 'OPENING',
  closing: 'CLOSING',
  intermediate: 'PARTIAL',
  unknown: '?',
};

const BADGE_LETTER: Readonly<Record<AlarmPriority, string>> = {
  none: '',
  caution: 'C',
  warning: 'W',
  alarm: 'A',
  emergency: 'E',
};

export function toValveView(inputs: ValveSymbolInputs): SymbolView {
  const stateText =
    inputs.positionState === 'intermediate' && inputs.actualPositionPercent !== undefined
      ? `${String(Math.round(inputs.actualPositionPercent))} %`
      : POSITION_TEXT[inputs.positionState];
  const hasAlert = inputs.alarmPriority !== 'none';
  const alarmText = hasAlert ? `, ${inputs.alarmPriority} ${inputs.alarmState}` : '';

  return {
    attributes: {
      'data-position': inputs.positionState,
      'data-alarm-priority': inputs.alarmPriority,
      'data-alarm-state': inputs.alarmState,
      'data-out-of-service': String(inputs.isOutOfService),
    },
    stateText,
    badgeLetter: BADGE_LETTER[inputs.alarmPriority],
    isBadgeFlashing: hasAlert && inputs.alarmState === 'unacknowledged',
    accessibleName: `Valve ${inputs.tagName}, ${stateText}${alarmText}`,
  };
}
```

- Write one such function per symbol type. Test it without a browser: give inputs, compare the result.
- Copy the template `templates/typescript-angular/synoptic-symbol.ts`; it passes the lint of the base.
- Use a `Record` keyed by the state type. The compiler then refuses a missing state.

## 3. Markup (symbol component)

Draw the symbol inline, in one `svg` element. Put the attributes of the view function on the root.

```html
<svg class="syn-symbol syn-valve" viewBox="0 0 64 80" role="img"
     aria-label="Valve V101, OPEN, warning unacknowledged"
     data-position="open" data-alarm-priority="warning" data-alarm-state="unacknowledged"
     data-out-of-service="false">
  <path class="syn-shape" d="M8 16 L32 32 L8 48 Z M56 16 L32 32 L56 48 Z" />
  <g class="syn-badge" transform="translate(44 0)">
    <path class="syn-badge-shape" d="M9 1 L17 17 L1 17 Z" />
    <text class="syn-badge-letter" x="9" y="15" text-anchor="middle">W</text>
  </g>
  <text class="syn-state" x="32" y="62" text-anchor="middle">OPEN</text>
  <text class="syn-tag" x="32" y="76" text-anchor="middle">V101</text>
</svg>
```

- Give every symbol type the same `viewBox` grid, so that symbols line up.
- Keep the same parts in the same order: shape, badge, state word, tag.
- Set `role="img"` and `aria-label` with the accessible name: type, tag, state word, alert. [S14]
- To rotate a symbol, rotate the shape only. Keep the texts horizontal. [S2]

Define the hatch pattern once per page:

```html
<svg width="0" height="0" aria-hidden="true">
  <defs>
    <pattern id="syn-hatch" width="6" height="6" patternUnits="userSpaceOnUse" patternTransform="rotate(45)">
      <line class="syn-hatch-line" x1="0" y1="0" x2="0" y2="6" />
    </pattern>
  </defs>
</svg>
```

## 4. Styles (symbol component)

```css
.syn-view {
  --syn-bg: #d9d9d9;
  --syn-line: #333333;
  --syn-text: #1a1a1a;
  --syn-fill-active: #ffffff;
  --syn-fill-inactive: #595959;
  --syn-alert-alarm: #c4001a;
  --syn-alert-warning: #e07000;
  --syn-alert-caution: #f2d000;
  --syn-invalid: #7a1fa2;
  background: var(--syn-bg);
}
.syn-view[data-palette="night"] {
  --syn-bg: #1a1a1a;
  --syn-line: #b3b3b3;
  --syn-text: #e6e6e6;
  --syn-fill-active: #e6e6e6;
  --syn-fill-inactive: #6b6b6b;
  --syn-alert-alarm: #ff4d4d;
  --syn-alert-warning: #ff9a1f;
  --syn-alert-caution: #ffe14d;
  --syn-invalid: #d48cff;
}
.syn-shape { fill: var(--syn-fill-inactive); stroke: var(--syn-line); stroke-width: 2; }
.syn-hatch-line { stroke: var(--syn-line); stroke-width: 2; }
.syn-symbol[data-position="open"] .syn-shape,
.syn-symbol[data-position="intermediate"] .syn-shape { fill: var(--syn-fill-active); }
.syn-symbol[data-position="opening"] .syn-shape,
.syn-symbol[data-position="closing"] .syn-shape { fill: url(#syn-hatch); }
.syn-symbol[data-position="unknown"] .syn-shape { fill: none; stroke: var(--syn-invalid); stroke-dasharray: 4 3; }
.syn-state, .syn-tag { fill: var(--syn-text); font: 10px sans-serif; }
.syn-badge { display: none; }
.syn-symbol:not([data-alarm-priority="none"]) .syn-badge { display: inline; }
.syn-badge-shape { stroke: var(--syn-line); stroke-width: 1.5; }
.syn-symbol[data-alarm-priority="caution"] .syn-badge-shape { fill: var(--syn-alert-caution); }
.syn-symbol[data-alarm-priority="warning"] .syn-badge-shape { fill: var(--syn-alert-warning); }
.syn-symbol[data-alarm-priority="alarm"] .syn-badge-shape,
.syn-symbol[data-alarm-priority="emergency"] .syn-badge-shape { fill: var(--syn-alert-alarm); }
.syn-view[data-flash-phase="off"] .syn-symbol[data-alarm-state="unacknowledged"] .syn-badge { opacity: 0.25; }
@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce) {
  .syn-symbol[data-alarm-state="unacknowledged"] .syn-badge-shape { stroke-width: 4; }
}
```

- The closed valve keeps the dark default fill. The open valve takes the bright fill.
- The badge always has a border in the line colour.
- The night palette changes only the variables. No symbol changes.
- With reduced motion, the badge does not flash. Its border is thicker instead. [S14] [S32]

## 5. Flash clock (symbol component)

Start one clock per view. It sets `data-flash-phase` on the view root. All badges then flash in phase, at 1 Hz.

```ts
export function startFlashClock(root: HTMLElement, periodMs = 1000): () => void {
  if (window.matchMedia('(prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)').matches) {
    root.dataset['flashPhase'] = 'on';
    return () => undefined;
  }
  let isOn = true;
  const timer = window.setInterval(() => {
    isOn = !isOn;
    root.dataset['flashPhase'] = isOn ? 'on' : 'off';
  }, periodMs / 2);
  return () => window.clearInterval(timer);
}
```

- Call the returned function when the view closes. It stops the clock.
- Never write a CSS animation inside a symbol. Two symbols would then flash out of phase.

## 6. Pitfalls (SVG symbol and use elements)

- A `symbol` element is never drawn by itself. Only a `use` element that points to it is drawn. [S34]
- A `use` element copies the shape into a tree that the page cannot reach. A style rule of the page does not select the parts of the copy. Only inherited properties get in. [S33]
- An attribute set inside the copied shape wins over the same attribute set on `use`. A `fill` on `use` has no effect when the shape already has a `fill`. [S33]
- `width` and `height` on `use` work only when the target is an `svg` or a `symbol` with a `viewBox`. [S33]
- A browser can refuse a `use` that points to a file from another origin. [S33]
- So: use `symbol` and `use` only for a part that never changes with the state. Draw every part that changes inline, as in section 3.
- The hatch does not show: check that the pattern with the id `syn-hatch` is in the same page.
- A graphic part needs a contrast of at least 3:1 with what touches it. Check each new colour in both palettes. [S14]

## 7. Final check (symbol component)

- Does the symbol take states and values only, and no colour?
- Is every colour a CSS variable?
- Is the state shown by a fill or a shape, and by a word?
- Does only the badge of an unacknowledged alarm or warning flash, from the view clock?
- Does the root have `role="img"` and an `aria-label` with the tag and the state word?

## Sources

- [S2] U.S. NRC, "Human-System Interface Design Review Guidelines", NUREG-0700 Rev. 3, 2020, sections 1.3.3 to 1.3.5 and 1.3.10, <https://www.nrc.gov/docs/ML2016/ML20162A214.pdf>, read 2026-11-12, U.S. federal publication, summarized.
- [S14] W3C, "Web Content Accessibility Guidelines (WCAG) 2.2", Recommendation of 2024-12-12, <https://www.w3.org/TR/WCAG22/>, read 2026-11-12, W3C Document License, summarized.
- [S32] MDN, "prefers-reduced-motion", <https://developer.mozilla.org/docs/Web/CSS/Reference/At-rules/@media/prefers-reduced-motion>, read 2026-11-12, license not read, summarized.
- [S33] MDN, "use, SVG element", <https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/SVG/Reference/Element/use>, read 2026-11-12, Creative Commons, summarized.
- [S34] MDN, "symbol, SVG element", <https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/SVG/Reference/Element/symbol>, read 2026-11-12, Creative Commons, summarized.

---
id: synoptics-symbol-property-naming
title: Symbol property naming
topic: synoptics
summary: Naming rules for the properties of a synoptic symbol and of its equipment model, with wrong and right examples.
tool: none
version: "none"
checked: 2026-11-13
sources: [S16, S17, S18, S19, S25, S26, S27, S28, S30, S31, S35, S36]

---

# Symbol property naming

Contents: 1. Casing · 2. Units · 3. Booleans · 4. States · 5. Command and feedback · 6. Common properties · 7. Final check · Sources

## 1. Casing (symbol property names)

- Write names in English. Use full words. Use nouns, not verbs.
- In TypeScript and in JSON: `camelCase`.
- In C#: `PascalCase`. [S30]
- Keep the same words on both sides. Let the JSON serializer convert the case. Never rename by hand. [S31]
- Write an acronym as a word: `tagId`, not `tagID`. [S18]

Right, in C#:

```csharp
public enum PositionState { Open, Closed, Opening, Closing, Intermediate, Unknown }

public sealed record ValveState(
    string TagName,
    PositionState PositionState,
    double CommandedPositionPercent,
    double ActualPositionPercent);

var options = new JsonSerializerOptions(JsonSerializerDefaults.Web);
options.Converters.Add(new JsonStringEnumConverter(JsonNamingPolicy.CamelCase));
// JSON: {"tagName":"V101","positionState":"open","commandedPositionPercent":100,"actualPositionPercent":100}
```

## 2. Units (symbol property names)

- A bare number with a physical unit carries its unit as the last word of its name. [S28]
- Take the suffix from the table below. Add a line to the table before you use a new suffix.
- Write the suffix as a word: first letter upper case, the rest lower case.
- Use `Percent` for a value from 0 to 100. Use `Ratio` for a value from 0 to 1.
- Use `Count` for a number of items. [S28]
- When the unit can change at run time, do not use a suffix. Use a value object with a `unit` field instead. Never do both. [S17] [S27] [S28]

| Quantity | Suffix | Example |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Pressure | `Bar`, `Mbar`, `Kpa` | `dischargePressureBar` |
| Temperature | `DegC` | `oilTemperatureDegC` |
| Temperature rise | `DegCPerMin` | `rateOfRiseThresholdDegCPerMin` |
| Level, opening, load | `Percent` | `tankLevelPercent` |
| Volume flow | `M3PerH`, `LPerMin` | `coolingFlowM3PerH` |
| Rotation speed | `Rpm` | `shaftSpeedRpm` |
| Ship or fluid speed | `Kn`, `MPerS` | `shipSpeedKn` |
| Voltage | `V` | `busVoltageV` |
| Current | `A` | `motorCurrentA` |
| Frequency | `Hz` | `busFrequencyHz` |
| Power | `Kw` | `activePowerKw` |
| Energy | `Kwh` | `suppliedEnergyKwh` |
| Length, depth | `M`, `Mm` | `keelDepthM` |
| Angle | `Deg` | `rudderAngleDeg` |
| Duration | `S`, `Ms` | `travelTimeS` |
| Mass | `Kg` | `fuelMassKg` |
| Volume | `M3`, `L` | `tankVolumeM3` |
| Small current | `Ma` | `loopCurrentMa` |
| Reactive and apparent power | `Kvar`, `Kva` | `reactivePowerKvar` |
| Battery charge | `Ah` | `batteryChargeAh` |
| Turn rate | `DegPerS` | `rateOfTurnDegPerS` |
| Torque | `Nm`, `Knm` | `shaftTorqueKnm` |
| Force | `N` | `rodForceN` |
| Run time | `H` | `runTimeH` |
| Inertia | `KgM2` | `shaftInertiaKgM2` |
| Ratio, count | `Ratio`, `Count` | `gearRatio`, `startCount` |
| Displacement, area | `Cm3`, `Cm2`, `M2` | `displacementCm3`, `pistonAreaCm2` |
| Low pressure | `Pa` | `filterDifferentialPressurePa` |
| Concentration | `Ppm` | `oilContentPpm` |
| Density, mass flow | `KgPerM3`, `KgPerS` | `densityKgPerM3`, `massFlowRateKgPerS` |
| Kinematic viscosity | `Cst` | `fuelViscosityCst` |

Write each unit with one spelling only: `Percent`, never `Pct`; `DegC`, never `C`; `M3PerH`, never `M3h`.

Wrong:

```ts
export interface PumpWrong {
  Pressure: number;
  pos: number;
  target: number;
}
```

Right:

```ts
export interface PumpRight {
  readonly dischargePressureBar: number;
  readonly dischargePressureSetpointBar: number;
  readonly shaftSpeedRpm: number;
  readonly startCount: number;
}
```

Right, when the unit can change:

```ts
export interface Measurement {
  readonly value: number;
  readonly unit: string;
}

export interface TankRight {
  readonly level: Measurement;
}
```

## 3. Booleans (symbol property names)

- Start a boolean with `is`, `has` or `can`. [S30]
- Name the true case in the affirmative: `isReady`, never `isNotReady`. [S30]
- Use a boolean only for a fact that stands alone.
- Never use two booleans for states that exclude each other. Use one state property (section 4).

Wrong:

```ts
export interface ValveWrong {
  open: boolean;
  closed: boolean;
  notReady: boolean;
}
```

Right:

```ts
export interface ValveRight {
  readonly positionState: 'open' | 'closed' | 'opening' | 'closing' | 'intermediate' | 'unknown';
  readonly isReady: boolean;
  readonly hasPositionFeedback: boolean;
}
```

## 4. States (symbol property names)

- Give one property to each group of states that exclude each other. [S16] [S19]
- End its name with `State`, `Mode`, `Location`, `Priority` or with the plain noun (`health`, `quality`).
- Write the values as `camelCase` words: `'maintenanceRequired'`.
- A switching device has a position with four values, as in IEC 61850: `intermediate` while it moves, `bad` when the two contacts disagree. [S36]
- Always include a value for the unknown case when the state comes from a sensor.

| Property | Values |
| --- | --- |
| `positionState` | `open`, `closed`, `opening`, `closing`, `intermediate`, `unknown` |
| `runState` | `running`, `stopped`, `starting`, `stopping`, `unknown` |
| `controlLocation` | `remote`, `local` |
| `controlMode` | `auto`, `manual` |
| `switchPosition` | `intermediate`, `open`, `closed`, `bad` (circuit breakers, switches, disconnectors) |
| `health` | `normal`, `failure`, `checkFunction`, `offSpec`, `maintenanceRequired` |
| `quality` | `good`, `uncertain`, `bad` |
| `alarmPriority` | `none`, `caution`, `warning`, `alarm`, `emergency` |
| `alarmState` | `normal`, `unacknowledged`, `acknowledged`, `returnedUnacknowledged`, `shelved`, `suppressed`, `outOfService` |

## 5. Command and feedback (symbol property names)

- Name what the system asks with `commanded`. Name what the equipment reports with `actual`. [S19]
- Use this pair for a position or a state: `commandedPositionPercent`, `actualPositionPercent`.
- For a regulated quantity, name the target with `Setpoint` before the unit. Name the measured value with the plain quantity.
- For alarm limits, use the four names of OPC UA before the unit: `HighHighLimit`, `HighLimit`, `LowLimit`, `LowLowLimit`; and `alarmDeadband` for the dead band. [S35]
- A symbol with one measured quantity can drop the quantity: `highLimitBar`.
- Order the words like this: location or role, quantity, qualifier, unit.

| Meaning | Name |
| --- | --- |
| Measured pressure at the discharge | `dischargePressureBar` |
| Pressure target | `dischargePressureSetpointBar` |
| High pressure limit | `dischargePressureHighLimitBar` |
| Very high pressure limit | `dischargePressureHighHighLimitBar` |
| Dead band of the alarms | `alarmDeadbandBar` |
| Position asked by the command | `commandedPositionPercent` |
| Position reported by the equipment | `actualPositionPercent` |
| State asked by the command | `commandedRunState` |
| State reported by the equipment | `runState` |

## 6. Common properties (every symbol)

Give these properties to every symbol. Add the properties of the equipment after them.

```ts
export interface CommonSymbolInputs {
  readonly tagName: string;
  readonly description?: string;
  readonly health: 'normal' | 'failure' | 'checkFunction' | 'offSpec' | 'maintenanceRequired';
  readonly quality: 'good' | 'uncertain' | 'bad';
  readonly controlLocation: 'remote' | 'local';
  readonly controlMode: 'auto' | 'manual';
  readonly isOutOfService: boolean;
  readonly isInhibited: boolean;
  readonly isSimulated: boolean;
  readonly alarmPriority: 'none' | 'caution' | 'warning' | 'alarm' | 'emergency';
  readonly alarmState:
    | 'normal'
    | 'unacknowledged'
    | 'acknowledged'
    | 'returnedUnacknowledged'
    | 'shelved'
    | 'suppressed'
    | 'outOfService';
}
```

- `tagName` is the identifier shown on the view. Show it as given. Never build it and never parse it. [S25] [S26]
- `health` follows the four standard diagnostic cases plus `normal`. [S16]

## 7. Final check (symbol property names)

- Does every bare number with a unit end with a suffix from the table?
- Does every boolean start with `is`, `has` or `can`, in the affirmative?
- Is every group of exclusive states one property, never several booleans?
- Are the words the same in TypeScript and in C#, with only the case changed?

## Sources

- [S16] OPC Foundation, "OPC 10000-100: Devices", v1.05, section 4.5.4, <https://reference.opcfoundation.org/specs/OPC-10000-100/4.5.4>, read 2026-11-12, OPC Foundation license, summarized.
- [S17] OPC Foundation, "OPC 10000-8: Data Access", v1.05.07, <https://reference.opcfoundation.org/specs/OPC-10000-8/full>, read 2026-11-12, OPC Foundation license, summarized.
- [S18] OPC Foundation, "OPC 11030: UA Modelling Best Practices", section 2.1.2, <https://reference.opcfoundation.org/specs/OPC-11030/2.1.2>, read 2026-11-12, OPC Foundation license, summarized.
- [S19] DNV, "DNV naming rule" (ISO 19848 Local ID), <https://docs.vista.dnv.com/docs/standards/dnv-naming-rule>, read 2026-11-12, license not stated, summarized.
- [S25] IEC, "IEC CDD, IEC 61987, property tag name", <https://cdd.iec.ch/cdd/iec61987/iec61987.nsf/0/1BE8325198445094C1258A7D0039DA99>, read 2026-11-12, all rights reserved, summarized.
- [S26] POSC Caesar Association, "CFIHOS RDL v2.0, property name", <https://posccaesar.org/cfihos/v2.0/CFIHOS-10000056>, read 2026-11-12, license not stated, summarized.
- [S27] Microsoft, "DTDL v4, QuantitativeTypes extension v2", <https://azure.github.io/opendigitaltwins-dtdl/DTDL/v4/DTDL.quantitativeTypes.v2.html>, read 2026-11-12, license not stated, summarized.

id: synoptics-synoptic-rules
title: Synoptic display rules
topic: synoptics
summary: Rules to lay out a synoptic view and to show equipment states, alerts and flashing.
tool: none
version: "none"
checked: 2026-11-12
sources: [S1, S2, S4, S5, S6, S11, S12, S14, S16, S32]
license: MIT (own words; standards and guides are cited by reference, never copied)
variant: files
---

# Synoptic display rules

Contents: 1. Layout · 2. Colour · 3. Equipment states · 4. Alerts · 5. Flashing and motion · 6. Text and numbers · 7. Final check · Sources

## 1. Layout (synoptic view)

- Show only what the operator's task needs. Draw no decoration, no 3D effect, no logo. [S2]
- Label every component. Start and end every flow line at a label or at a labelled component. [S2]
- Mark the flow direction with arrowheads. [S2]
- Do not overlap lines. Draw each crossing so that it cannot be read as a junction. [S2]
- Make what changes (a valve position, a value) stand out more than what never changes (a pipe, a tank outline). [S2]
- Put each value next to the equipment it belongs to. [S2]
- Give every view a short title at the top. [S2]
- Keep the view sparse. Use at most half of the area. Use at most a quarter on a view that is mostly text. [S2]
- Keep the same place for the same thing on every view. [S6]
- Build four levels of views and link them: overview, system, equipment detail, diagnostic. Put no command on the overview. [S1]

## 2. Colour (synoptic view)

- Use a plain background with no pattern. [S2]
- Provide a day palette and a night palette. At night, draw light items on a dark background. [S6]
- Take every colour from a CSS variable. Never write a colour value inside a symbol.
- Use few colours. Give one meaning to one colour. [S2]
- Draw equipment, pipes and normal values in neutral greys. Use bright colour only for an abnormal situation. [S1]
- Reserve red, orange and yellow for alerts and for invalid data. [S5] [S6]
- Never use red or green to mean open or closed, running or stopped. People from different trades read them in opposite ways. [S2]
- Never use colour alone. Add a shape, a pattern or a word that gives the same information. [S2] [S6] [S14]
- Keep a contrast of at least 3:1 between a graphic part and what touches it. Keep at least 4.5:1 for text. [S14]
- In a normal situation, highlight at most about one tenth of the view. [S2]

Wrong:

```css
.syn-valve[data-position="open"] .syn-shape { fill: green; }
.syn-valve[data-position="closed"] .syn-shape { fill: red; }
```

Right:

```css
.syn-valve[data-position="open"] .syn-shape { fill: var(--syn-fill-active); }
.syn-valve[data-position="closed"] .syn-shape { fill: var(--syn-fill-inactive); }
/* The symbol also shows the word OPEN or CLOSED. */
```

## 3. Equipment states (synoptic symbol)

Show each state with a fill or a shape, and with a word. Use this table.

| State | Fill or shape | Word or badge |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Open, running, on | Bright fill | `OPEN`, `RUN`, `ON` |
| Closed, stopped, off | Dark fill | `CLOSED`, `STOP`, `OFF` |
| Opening, closing, starting, stopping | Hatched fill | `OPENING`, `CLOSING`, `STARTING`, `STOPPING` |
| Partly open | Bright fill | The position, for example `45 %` |
| Unknown position, invalid value | No fill, dashed outline | `?` |
| Fault, or position different from the command | Alert badge (section 4) | The alert text |
| Local control | Normal fill | Badge `LOC` |
| Manual mode | Normal fill | Badge `MAN` |
| Out of service, locked out | Cross-hatch over the symbol | Badge `OOS` |
| Inhibited, interlocked | Normal fill | Badge `INH` |
| Simulated or forced value | Normal fill | Badge `SIM` |
| Device diagnostic | Normal fill | Badge `FAIL`, `CHECK`, `OFFSPEC` or `MAINT` |

- Use only two brightness levels: bright and dark. [S2]
- Show a mode badge only when the mode is not the normal mode of the equipment.
- Use the four standard diagnostic cases: failure, function check, out of specification, maintenance required. A forced or substitute value is a function check. Local operation is a function check. [S16]
- Never show an invalid or doubtful value as a good value. Mark it. [S6]
- Write the words in the language of the project screens. The words above are the English defaults.

## 4. Alerts (synoptic symbol and alert list)

Use four priorities. [S4] [S5]

| Priority | Meaning | Not acknowledged | Acknowledged | Sound |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `emergency` | Immediate danger to life or to the ship | Flashing | Steady | Follow the emergency signal rules of the project |
| `alarm` | Needs attention and action now | Flashing | Steady until the cause is gone | Until acknowledged |
| `warning` | Needs attention now, no action yet | Flashing | Steady until the cause is gone | One short signal |
| `caution` | Needs more attention than usual | Steady | Steady | None |

- Show an alert with a badge next to the equipment that raised it. [S1]
- Give each priority its own outer shape, its own letter and its own colour. Defaults: `E` in an octagon, red; `A` in a square, red; `W` in a triangle, orange; `C` in a circle, yellow. [S1] [S2]
- Draw a dark border around every badge. Orange and yellow do not contrast enough with a light background.
- Use the alert colours and the badge shapes for alerts only. [S1] [S2]
- Acknowledge alerts one by one. Never acknowledge a group in one action. [S4]
- For a shelved, suppressed or out-of-service alarm: show no alert colour and no flashing. A small neutral marker is allowed. Provide a list of these alarms. [S11] [S12]
- In the alert list: show at least 20 alerts at once. Sort by priority, then by time. Keep a history with the times raised, acknowledged and cleared. [S4]
- From an alert, open the view of its source in one action. [S11]

## 5. Flashing and motion (synoptic symbol)

- Flash only an alarm or a warning that is not acknowledged. Flash nothing else. [S4] [S6]
- Use one rate: 1 Hz, bright for half of the period. Stay between 0.5 Hz and 1.5 Hz. [S5]
- Never flash more than three times per second. [S14]
- Flash the badge only. Never flash a text that must be read. Never flash the whole symbol. [S2]
- Flash between two visible levels. Never hide the item. [S2]
- Flash all the badges of a view in phase, from one clock.
- When the user asks for reduced motion, stop the flashing and draw a thick border instead. [S14] [S32]
- Add no other animation: no rotating pump, no moving fluid, no growing bar. [S2]

## 6. Text and numbers (synoptic symbol)

- Write every label and every number horizontally, even when the symbol is rotated. [S2]
- Put the label close to its symbol. [S2]
- Use the same label for the same item on every view. [S2]
- Spell words out. Abbreviate only when space forces it. Never use one abbreviation for two words. [S2]
- Show each value with its unit. Use the same unit for the same quantity on every view. [S6]
- Show only the digits the task needs. [S2]
- Refresh a value that must be read at most once per second. [S2]
- Add an arrow when the operator must see the direction of change. [S2]

## 7. Final check (synoptic view)

- Is every colour information doubled by a shape, a pattern or a word?
- Is red or green used for open, closed, running or stopped? It must not be.
- Does anything flash other than an unacknowledged alarm or warning? It must not.
- Does every colour come from a CSS variable?
- Does every flow line start and end at a label or at a labelled component?

## Sources

- [S1] B. R. Hollifield, "The High Performance HMI", ISA Interchange, undated, <https://blog.isa.org/the-high-performance-hmi>, read 2026-11-12, license not stated, summarized.
- [S2] U.S. NRC, "Human-System Interface Design Review Guidelines", NUREG-0700 Rev. 3, 2020, sections 1.2.8, 1.3.2 to 1.3.10, 1.4, 1.5, <https://www.nrc.gov/docs/ML2016/ML20162A214.pdf>, read 2026-11-12, U.S. federal publication, summarized.
- [S4] IMO, Resolution MSC.302(87), "Performance standards for bridge alert management", 2010, <https://wwwcdn.imo.org/localresources/en/KnowledgeCentre/IndexofIMOResolutions/MSCResolutions/MSC.302(87).pdf>, read 2026-11-12, license not stated, summarized.
- [S5] IMO, Resolution A.1021(26), "Code on Alerts and Indicators, 2009", <https://wwwcdn.imo.org/localresources/en/KnowledgeCentre/IndexofIMOResolutions/AssemblyDocuments/A.1021(26).pdf>, read 2026-11-12, license not stated, summarized.
- [S6] IMO, Resolution MSC.191(79), "Performance standards for the presentation of navigation-related information on shipborne navigational displays", 2004, <https://wwwcdn.imo.org/localresources/en/KnowledgeCentre/IndexofIMOResolutions/MSCResolutions/MSC.191(79).pdf>, read 2026-11-12, license not stated, summarized.
- [S11] Siemens Industry, "Setting a new standard in alarm management", 2021, <https://assets.new.siemens.com/siemens/assets/api/uuid:234f1026-298f-49dc-8961-0c5223c38588/white-paper-alarm-management-2021-final.pdf>, read 2026-11-12, all rights reserved, summarized.
- [S12] Siemens Ltd, "Improving alarm management with ISA-18.2: Part 2", Process Online, 2014, <https://www.processonline.com.au/content/software-it/article/improving-alarm-management-with-isa-18-2-part-2-1141142642>, read 2026-11-12, license not stated, summarized.
- [S14] W3C, "Web Content Accessibility Guidelines (WCAG) 2.2", Recommendation of 2024-12-12, <https://www.w3.org/TR/WCAG22/>, read 2026-11-12, W3C Document License, summarized.

- [S32] MDN, "prefers-reduced-motion", <https://developer.mozilla.org/docs/Web/CSS/Reference/At-rules/@media/prefers-reduced-motion>, read 2026-11-12, license not read, summarized.

# Lint TypeScript et Angular

Les règles de lint que l'outil ne corrige pas seul et que les modèles enfreignent le plus. Chaque
règle a un exemple faux et un exemple juste, vérifiés par les tests de la base avec ESLint et le
compilateur. La configuration à copier est dans `templates/typescript-angular/`.

| Fichier | Ce qu'il contient |
| --- | --- |
| `ts-types.md` | `any` et `unknown`, conversions forcées (`as`), `!` après une valeur, erreurs lancées. |
| `ts-null-and-conditions.md` | Accès par index, conditions inutiles, `switch` complet, chaînes de modèle, `??`. |
| `ts-async.md` | Promesses : toujours les traiter, attendre avant de tester, `async` seulement avec `await`. |
| `angular-components-v22.md` | `inject()`, entrées et sorties à signaux, méthodes de cycle de vie, `host`, composants autonomes. |
| `angular-templates-v22.md` | `@if` et `@for`, liaisons de classe, type des boutons, clavier, listes vides. |
| `angular-rxjs.md` | Fin des abonnements avec le composant, flux transformés en signaux. |
| `ts-angular-strict-format.md` | Ordre des membres d'une classe, et ce que la commande de correction range seule. |

Les deux commandes : `npm run fix` corrige tout ce que l'outil sait corriger, puis `npm run check`
liste ce qui reste. `STRICT_FORMAT=0 npm run check` contrôle sans le formatage strict.

---
id: typescript-lint-angular-components-v22
title: Angular 22 components
topic: typescript-lint
summary: Rules for inject(), signal inputs and outputs, lifecycle methods, host bindings and standalone components. Angular 22.
tool: angular-eslint
version: "22.6"
checked: 2026-11-13

# Angular 22 components

Contents: 1. inject() · 2. Signal inputs · 3. Outputs · 4. Lifecycle methods · 5. Host bindings · 6. Output names · 7. Standalone · Sources

In Angular 22, components are standalone and use OnPush by default: never write `standalone: true`
and never write `changeDetection` unless the project asks for it. [S2]

## 1. Inject with inject(), not with the constructor (angular-eslint prefer-inject)

Get a service with a field: `private readonly api = inject(ValveApi);`. Never list services in the constructor. [S1]

Wrong:

```ts
@Injectable({ providedIn: 'root' })
export class PumpStore {
  public constructor(private readonly http: HttpClient) {}
}
```

Right, with `@Service()`, the service decorator of Angular 22:

```ts
@Service()
export class PumpStore {
  private readonly http = inject(HttpClient);

  public load(): Observable<unknown> {
    return this.http.get<unknown>('/api/pumps');
  }
}
```

## 2. Use signal inputs (angular-eslint prefer-signals)

Declare an input with `input()` or `input.required()`, never with `@Input()`. Read it as a function in the template: `label()`. [S1]

Wrong:

```ts
@Component({ selector: 'app-pump-label', template: '{{ label }}' })
export class PumpLabel {
  @Input() public label = '';
}
```

Right:

```ts
@Component({ selector: 'app-pump-label', template: '{{ label() }}' })
export class PumpLabel {
  public readonly label = input('');
}
```

## 3. Use output() (angular-eslint prefer-output-emitter-ref)

Declare an output with `output<Type>()`, never with `@Output()` and `EventEmitter`. [S1]

Wrong:

```ts
@Component({
  selector: 'app-stop-button',
  template: '<button type="button" (click)="stopped.emit()">Stop</button>',
})
export class StopButton {
  @Output() public readonly stopped = new EventEmitter<void>();
}
```

Right:

```ts
@Component({
  selector: 'app-stop-button',
  template: '<button type="button" (click)="stopped.emit()">Stop</button>',
})
export class StopButton {
  public readonly stopped = output();
}
```

## 4. Keep lifecycle methods synchronous (angular-eslint no-async-lifecycle-method)

Never write `async ngOnInit()`. Angular does not wait for it. Start the work with `void`, in a separate method. [S1]

Wrong:

```ts
@Component({ selector: 'app-valve-count', template: '{{ count() }}' })
export class ValveCount implements OnInit {
  protected readonly count = signal(0);
  private readonly api = inject(ValveApi);

  public async ngOnInit(): Promise<void> {
    this.count.set((await this.api.loadValves()).length);
  }
}
```

Right:

```ts
@Component({ selector: 'app-valve-count', template: '{{ count() }}' })
export class ValveCount implements OnInit {
  protected readonly count = signal(0);
  private readonly api = inject(ValveApi);

  public ngOnInit(): void {
    void this.load();
  }

  private async load(): Promise<void> {
    const valves = await this.api.loadValves();

    this.count.set(valves.length);
  }
}
```

## 5. Bind host events in the metadata (angular-eslint prefer-host-metadata-property)

Write host events and bindings in `host: { … }` of `@Component`, never with `@HostListener` or `@HostBinding`. [S1]

Wrong:

```ts
@Component({ selector: 'app-escape-stop', template: '' })
export class EscapeStop {
  public readonly stopped = output();

  @HostListener('window:keydown.escape')
  protected stop(): void {
    this.stopped.emit();
  }
}
```

Right:

```ts
@Component({
  selector: 'app-escape-stop',
  template: '',
  host: { '(window:keydown.escape)': 'stop()' },
})
export class EscapeStop {
  public readonly stopped = output();

  protected stop(): void {
    this.stopped.emit();
  }
}
```

## 6. Name an output after the event, without "on" (angular-eslint no-output-on-prefix)

Name an output `stopped` or `valveSelected`, never `onStop`. [S1]

Wrong:

```ts
@Component({ selector: 'app-pump-stop', template: '' })
export class PumpStop {
  public readonly onStop = output();
}
```

Right:

```ts
@Component({ selector: 'app-pump-stop', template: '' })
export class PumpStop {
  public readonly stopped = output();
}
```

## 7. Keep components standalone (angular-eslint prefer-standalone)

Never write `standalone: false`. Import what the template needs in `imports: [ … ]` of the component. [S1] [S2]

Wrong:

```ts
@Component({ selector: 'app-tank-view', standalone: false, template: '' })
export class TankView {}
```

Right:

```ts
@Component({ selector: 'app-tank-view', template: '' })
export class TankView {}
```

## Sources

- [S1] angular-eslint, rules of @angular-eslint/eslint-plugin, version 22.6, <https://github.com/angular-eslint/angular-eslint/tree/main/packages/eslint-plugin/docs/rules>, read 2026-11-13, MIT, summarized.
- [S2] Google, "Angular coding style guide" and "Components", Angular 22, <https://angular.dev/style-guide>, read 2026-11-13, CC BY 4.0, summarized.

=====BASE-EXPORT-1-571e463b6744===== FILE 1/1 c9ea32fa635cf793009146b01cdbdd400d32d89c713c82761649852b2b3eb803 RAW code/docs/typescript-lint/angular-rxjs.md
---
id: typescript-lint-angular-rxjs
title: Angular and RxJS
topic: typescript-lint
summary: Rules to end subscriptions with the component and to turn streams into signals. Angular 22, RxJS 7.
tool: angular-eslint
version: "22.6"
checked: 2026-11-13
sources: [S1, S2]
license: MIT, examples written for this base
variant: files
---

# Angular and RxJS

Contents: 1. Streams as signals · 2. takeUntilDestroyed · 3. Pitfalls · Sources

## 1. Turn a stream into a signal (angular-eslint no-implicit-take-until-destroyed)

To show the last value of a stream, use `toSignal()` in a field. It ends the subscription with the component. [S2]

Why: `takeUntilDestroyed()` without an argument works only during the creation of the component. Called in `ngOnInit` or in a method, it throws an error.

Wrong:

```ts
@Component({ selector: 'app-alarm-banner', template: '{{ last() }}' })
export class AlarmBanner implements OnInit {
  protected readonly last = signal('');
  private readonly alarms = inject(AlarmFeed);

  public ngOnInit(): void {
    this.alarms.messages$.pipe(takeUntilDestroyed()).subscribe((message) => {
      this.last.set(message);
    });
  }
}
```

Right:

```ts
@Component({ selector: 'app-alarm-banner', template: '{{ last() }}' })
export class AlarmBanner {
  private readonly alarms = inject(AlarmFeed);
  protected readonly last = toSignal(this.alarms.messages$, { initialValue: '' });
}
```

## 2. Subscribe in a field when you must (angular-eslint no-implicit-take-until-destroyed)

When a stream must run code, subscribe while the component is created: in a field or the constructor, with `takeUntilDestroyed()`. [S1] [S2]

Wrong:

```ts
@Component({ selector: 'app-alarm-sound', template: '' })
export class AlarmSound {
  private readonly alarms = inject(AlarmFeed);

  public start(): void {
    this.alarms.messages$.pipe(takeUntilDestroyed()).subscribe(() => {
      this.ring();
    });
  }

  private ring(): void {
    this.rang.set(true);
  }

  private readonly rang = signal(false);
}
```

Right:

```ts
@Component({ selector: 'app-alarm-sound', template: '' })
export class AlarmSound {
  private readonly alarms = inject(AlarmFeed);
  private readonly rang = signal(false);

  public constructor() {
    this.alarms.messages$.pipe(takeUntilDestroyed()).subscribe(() => {
      this.rang.set(true);
    });
  }
}
```

## 3. Pitfalls (RxJS in Angular)

- A `subscribe()` inside another `subscribe()`: no lint rule catches it. Chain the streams with `switchMap` instead.
- `toPromise()` is deprecated in RxJS 7: use `firstValueFrom()` or `lastValueFrom()`.
- Keep state in signals; keep streams for events that happen over time (messages, timers, sockets).

## Sources

- [S1] angular-eslint, "no-implicit-take-until-destroyed", version 22.6, <https://github.com/angular-eslint/angular-eslint/blob/main/packages/eslint-plugin/docs/rules/no-implicit-take-until-destroyed.md>, read 2026-11-13, MIT, summarized.
- [S2] Google, "RxJS interop with Angular signals", Angular 22, <https://angular.dev/ecosystem/rxjs-interop>, read 2026-11-13, CC BY 4.0, summarized.

---
id: typescript-lint-angular-templates-v22
title: Angular 22 templates
topic: typescript-lint
summary: Rules for control flow, class bindings, buttons, keyboard access and empty lists in templates. Angular 22.
tool: angular-eslint
version: "22.6"
checked: 2026-11-13

# Angular 22 templates

Contents: 1. Control flow · 2. Class bindings · 3. Button type · 4. Click and keyboard · 5. Empty lists · Sources

## 1. Use @if and @for, never *ngIf and *ngFor (angular-eslint template prefer-control-flow)

Write `@if`, `@for` and `@switch` blocks. `*ngIf`, `*ngFor` and `*ngSwitch` are old. Each `@for` needs a `track` expression. [S1] [S2]

Wrong:

```html
<ul>
  <li *ngFor="let valve of valves">{{ valve.label }}</li>
</ul>
```

Right:

```html
<ul>
  @for (valve of valves(); track valve.id) {
    <li>{{ valve.label }}</li>
  }
</ul>
```

## 2. Bind one class at a time (angular-eslint template prefer-class-binding)

Write `[class.fault]="isFault()"`, never `[ngClass]`. Write `[style.width.px]`, never `[ngStyle]`. [S1]

Wrong:

```html
<li [ngClass]="{ fault: isFault() }">Valve</li>
```

Right:

```html
<li [class.fault]="isFault()">Valve</li>
```

## 3. Give every button its type (angular-eslint template button-has-type)

Write `type="button"` on each button that is not the submit button of a form. [S1]

Why: a button without type submits the form around it.

Wrong:

```html
<button (click)="stop()">Stop</button>
```

Right:

```html
<button type="button" (click)="stop()">Stop</button>
```

## 4. Put a click on a button, never on a plain element (angular-eslint template click-events-have-key-events)

Put `(click)` on a `<button>` or a link. A click on a `<li>` or a `<div>` cannot be reached with the keyboard. [S1]

Wrong:

```html
<li (click)="select()">Valve V-101</li>
```

Right:

```html
<li>
  <button type="button" (click)="select()">Valve V-101</button>
</li>
```

## 5. Show an empty list with @empty (angular-eslint template prefer-at-empty)

For the text of an empty list, write an `@empty` block inside `@for`, not a second `@if`. [S1]

Wrong:

```html
@for (valve of valves(); track valve.id) {
  <p>{{ valve.label }}</p>
}
@if (valves().length === 0) {
  <p>No valve</p>
}
```

Right:

```html
@for (valve of valves(); track valve.id) {
  <p>{{ valve.label }}</p>
} @empty {
  <p>No valve</p>
}
```

## Sources

- [S1] angular-eslint, rules of @angular-eslint/eslint-plugin-template, version 22.6, <https://github.com/angular-eslint/angular-eslint/tree/main/packages/eslint-plugin-template/docs/rules>, read 2026-11-13, MIT, summarized.
- [S2] Google, "Control flow", Angular 22, <https://angular.dev/guide/templates/control-flow>, read 2026-11-13, CC BY 4.0, summarized.

# Strict formatting

Contents: 1. Member order · 2. What the fix command does · 3. The switch · Sources

## 1. Write fields, then the constructor, then methods (typescript-eslint member-ordering)

In a class, write all fields first, then the constructor, then the methods. The fix command cannot move them for you. [S1]

Wrong:

```ts
export class TankGauge {
  public percent(level: number): number {
    return (level / this.capacity) * 100;
  }

  private readonly capacity = 40;
}
```

Right:

```ts
export class TankGauge {
  private readonly capacity = 40;

  public percent(level: number): number {
    return (level / this.capacity) * 100;
  }
}
```

## 2. What the fix command orders for you (ESLint strict formatting)

`npm run fix` sorts imports and exports, adds braces to every `if`, adds a blank line before each `return` and between class members, and sorts the keys of Angular decorators. Prettier then sets spaces and line breaks. Never do these by hand: run the command. [S2]

## 3. The switch (STRICT_FORMAT)

The strict rules are on by default. `STRICT_FORMAT=0 npm run check` runs the check without them. Never turn them off to hide an error.

## Sources

- [S1] typescript-eslint, "member-ordering", version 8, <https://typescript-eslint.io/rules/member-ordering>, read 2026-11-13, MIT, summarized.
- [S2] Rules of `eslint.strict-format.mjs` in `templates/typescript-angular/` of this base: ESLint core, `@stylistic/eslint-plugin` 5.10, `eslint-plugin-simple-import-sort` 14, angular-eslint 22.6, all MIT.

topic: typescript-lint
summary: Rules to handle every promise, await before a test, and use async only with await. typescript-eslint 8.

# TypeScript promises

Contents: 1. Handle every promise · 2. Await before you test · 3. async only with await · 4. Pitfalls · Sources

## 1. Handle every promise (typescript-eslint no-floating-promises)

Handle each promise: `await` it, `return` it, or mark it with `void` when its result does not matter. [S1]

Why: a promise that nobody handles hides its error.

Wrong:

```ts
export class ValveSaver {
  private readonly router = inject(Router);

  public save(): void {
    this.router.navigate(['/valves']);
  }
}
```

Right:

```ts
export class ValveSaver {
  private readonly router = inject(Router);

  public async save(): Promise<void> {
    await this.router.navigate(['/valves']);
  }
}
```

For several promises, join them with `Promise.all()`, then handle the result. [S1]

## 2. Await a promise before you test it (typescript-eslint no-misused-promises)

Never put a promise in a condition. Await it first. [S2]

Why: a promise is always truthy, so the test is always true.

Wrong:

```ts
export class ValveCloser {
  private readonly api = inject(ValveApi);

  public async closeIfOpen(id: string): Promise<void> {
    if (this.api.isOpen(id)) {
      await this.api.close(id);
    }
  }
}
```

Right:

```ts
export class ValveCloser {
  private readonly api = inject(ValveApi);

  public async closeIfOpen(id: string): Promise<void> {
    if (await this.api.isOpen(id)) {
      await this.api.close(id);
    }
  }
}
```

## 3. Use async only with await (typescript-eslint require-await)

Mark a function `async` only when it contains `await`. [S3]

Why: an `async` function without `await` wraps a plain value in a promise for nothing.

Wrong:

```ts
export async function valveLabel(valve: Valve): Promise<string> {
  return valve.label;
}
```

Right:

```ts
export function valveLabel(valve: Valve): string {
  return valve.label;
}
```

## 4. Pitfalls (typescript-eslint promise rules)

- `void` does not handle the error of the promise. Use `void` only when the error does not matter. Else use `await` inside `try`. [S1]
- An event handler of a template that calls an `async` method: write `void this.save()` in a method that returns `void`.
- Never add a comment to switch a promise rule off: such comments have no effect in this project.

## Sources

- [S1] typescript-eslint, "no-floating-promises", version 8, <https://typescript-eslint.io/rules/no-floating-promises>, read 2026-11-13, MIT, summarized.
- [S2] typescript-eslint, "no-misused-promises", version 8, <https://typescript-eslint.io/rules/no-misused-promises>, read 2026-11-13, MIT, summarized.
- [S3] typescript-eslint, "require-await", version 8, <https://typescript-eslint.io/rules/require-await>, read 2026-11-13, MIT, summarized.

topic: typescript-lint

tool: typescript-eslint, TypeScript

# TypeScript null checks and conditions

Contents: 1. Index access · 2. Needless conditions · 3. Complete switch · 4. Template strings · 5. Nullish values · Sources

## 1. A value read by its index can be undefined (TypeScript noUncheckedIndexedAccess)

With `noUncheckedIndexedAccess`, `list[0]` has the type `Item | undefined`. Check it before you use it. [S1]

Why: an empty array or a missing key gives `undefined`, not an item.

Wrong:

```ts
export function firstId(valves: readonly Valve[]): string {
  return valves[0].id;
}
```

Right:

```ts
export function firstId(valves: readonly Valve[]): string | undefined {
  return valves[0]?.id;
}
```

## 2. Test only what can change (typescript-eslint no-unnecessary-condition)

Never test a value that the types say is always set, or always true. Fix the type instead. [S2]

Why: a needless test hides a wrong type, and the other branch is dead code.

Wrong:

```ts
export function describeValve(valve: Valve): string {
  if (valve !== undefined) {
    return valve.label;
  }

  return 'unknown';
}
```

Right:

```ts
export function describeValve(valve: Valve | undefined): string {
  return valve?.label ?? 'unknown';
}
```

## 3. Handle every case of a union in a switch (typescript-eslint switch-exhaustiveness-check)

A `switch` on a union type has one `case` per member. Add no `default` that hides a new member. [S3]

Why: when a new state is added, the compiler then shows every switch to update.

Wrong:

```ts
export function stateText(state: ValveState): string {
  switch (state) {
    case 'open':
      return 'Open';
    case 'closed':
      return 'Closed';
    default:
      return '';
  }
}
```

Right:

```ts
export function stateText(state: ValveState): string {
  switch (state) {
    case 'open':
      return 'Open';
    case 'closed':
      return 'Closed';
    case 'fault':
      return 'Fault';
  }
}
```

## 4. Put only strings and numbers in a template string (typescript-eslint restrict-template-expressions)

In `${…}`, put a string or a number. Give a fallback for a value that can be `undefined`. [S4]

Why: `${undefined}` prints the word "undefined" on the screen.

Wrong:

```ts
export function tankLine(tank: string | undefined, percent: number): string {
  return `${tank} ${percent} %`;
}
```

Right:

```ts
export function tankLine(tank: string | undefined, percent: number): string {
  return `${tank ?? 'unknown tank'} ${percent} %`;
}
```

## 5. Use ?? for a missing value (typescript-eslint prefer-nullish-coalescing)

Use `??` to replace `undefined` or `null`. Use `||` only to replace every false value on purpose. [S5]

Why: `||` also replaces `0` and the empty string, which are real values.

Wrong:

```ts
export function labelOf(label: string | undefined): string {
  return label || 'none';
}
```

Right:

```ts
export function labelOf(label: string | undefined): string {
  return label ?? 'none';
}
```

## Sources

- [S1] Microsoft, "TSConfig Reference: noUncheckedIndexedAccess", TypeScript 6.0, <https://www.typescriptlang.org/tsconfig/#noUncheckedIndexedAccess>, read 2026-11-13, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S2] typescript-eslint, "no-unnecessary-condition", version 8, <https://typescript-eslint.io/rules/no-unnecessary-condition>, read 2026-11-13, MIT, summarized.
- [S3] typescript-eslint, "switch-exhaustiveness-check", version 8, <https://typescript-eslint.io/rules/switch-exhaustiveness-check>, read 2026-11-13, MIT, summarized.
- [S4] typescript-eslint, "restrict-template-expressions", version 8, <https://typescript-eslint.io/rules/restrict-template-expressions>, read 2026-11-13, MIT, summarized.
- [S5] typescript-eslint, "prefer-nullish-coalescing", version 8, <https://typescript-eslint.io/rules/prefer-nullish-coalescing>, read 2026-11-13, MIT, summarized.

topic: typescript-lint

# TypeScript types without any

Contents: 1. unknown, never any · 2. Check the shape · 3. Never write ! · 4. Throw an Error · Pitfalls · Sources

## 1. Use unknown, never any (typescript-eslint no-explicit-any)

Never write `any`. Write `unknown`, then check the value before you use it. [S1]

Why: `any` turns the type checks off, so every later error goes unseen.

Wrong:

```ts
export function readLevel(sample: any): number {
  return sample.level;
}
```

Right:

```ts
export function readLevel(sample: unknown): number {
  if (typeof sample === 'object' && sample !== null && 'level' in sample) {
    return Number(sample.level);
  }

  return 0;
}
```

## 2. Check the shape, never force a type (typescript-eslint no-unsafe-type-assertion)

Never write `as <Type>` to give a value a narrower type. Check its shape with a type guard. [S2]

Why: `as` tells the compiler to trust you. A wrong shape then breaks at run time, far away.

Wrong:

```ts
export function toValves(body: unknown): Valve[] {
  return body as Valve[];
}
```

Right:

```ts
export function toValves(body: unknown): Valve[] {
  if (!Array.isArray(body) || !body.every(isValve)) {
    throw new Error('The valve list has a wrong shape.');
  }

  return body;
}
```

## 3. Check for undefined, never write ! (typescript-eslint no-non-null-assertion)

Never write `!` after a value. Use `?.` and `??`, or test for `undefined`. [S3]

Why: `!` hides a value that can be missing. The code then fails on the first missing value.

Wrong:

```ts
export function firstOpenLabel(valves: readonly Valve[]): string {
  return valves.find((valve) => valve.state === 'open')!.label;
}
```

Right:

```ts
export function firstOpenLabel(valves: readonly Valve[]): string {
  return valves.find((valve) => valve.state === 'open')?.label ?? 'none';
}
```

## 4. Throw an Error object (typescript-eslint only-throw-error)

Throw only `Error` objects: `throw new Error('message')`. [S4]

Why: a thrown string has no stack trace, and `catch` code expects an `Error`.

Wrong:

```ts
export function requireOpen(valve: Valve): void {
  if (valve.state !== 'open') {
    throw 'The valve is closed.';
  }
}
```

Right:

```ts
export function requireOpen(valve: Valve): void {
  if (valve.state !== 'open') {
    throw new Error('The valve is closed.');
  }
}
```

## 5. Pitfalls (typescript-eslint unsafe rules)

- `Unsafe member access .x on an any value` or `Unsafe return of a value of type any`: an `any` came in earlier. Find where it came from and type it as `unknown` there.
- A value in `catch (error)` has the type `unknown`. Check it with `error instanceof Error` before you read `error.message`.
- `JSON.parse` and an HTTP body give an untyped value: read them as `unknown`, then check them.

## Sources

- [S1] typescript-eslint, "no-explicit-any", version 8, <https://typescript-eslint.io/rules/no-explicit-any>, read 2026-11-13, MIT, summarized.
- [S2] typescript-eslint, "no-unsafe-type-assertion", version 8, <https://typescript-eslint.io/rules/no-unsafe-type-assertion>, read 2026-11-13, MIT, summarized.
- [S3] typescript-eslint, "no-non-null-assertion", version 8, <https://typescript-eslint.io/rules/no-non-null-assertion>, read 2026-11-13, MIT, summarized.
- [S4] typescript-eslint, "only-throw-error", version 8, <https://typescript-eslint.io/rules/only-throw-error>, read 2026-11-13, MIT, summarized.

# Node.js versions and built-in APIs

Contents: 1. Versions · 2. Running a TypeScript file · 3. Built-in APIs · Sources

## 1. Versions (Node.js LTS)

| Node.js | Released | State on 2026-11-12 | Angular that accepts it |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 26 | 2026-05-05 | LTS | 22 |
| 24 | 2025-05-06 | LTS | 20, 21; 22 from 24.15 |
| 22 | 2024-04-24 | LTS | 18 to 21; 22 from 22.22.3 |
| 20 | 2023-04-17 | End of life | 17 to 21 |

Use only LTS versions in production. From version 27, one major version a year, and every version becomes LTS. [S1] [S2]

## 2. Running a TypeScript file directly (Node.js type stripping)

From Node.js 22.18, `node file.ts` runs a TypeScript file with no option. Node.js replaces the types by spaces: it checks no type and ignores `tsconfig.json`. [S3]

- Refused: `enum`, a `namespace` with code, parameter properties in a constructor, decorators, `.tsx` files.
- Write the extension in each import: `import { x } from './file.ts'`. Write `import type` for a type.
- A `.ts` file under `node_modules` is refused.

Wrong:

```ts
enum Mode { Fix, Check }
import { checkBase } from './check-base';
```

Right:

```ts
const MODES = ['fix', 'check'] as const;
import { checkBase } from './check-base.ts';
```

## 3. Built-in APIs that replace packages (Node.js 22 to 26)

| API | Since | Replaces |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `node --watch` | 22, stable | A tool that restarts on change |
| `glob`, `globSync` in `node:fs` | 22 | A file pattern package |
| `WebSocket` (client) | 22 | A WebSocket client package |
| `fetch`, `util.parseArgs`, `node:test` and `node --test` | 22 at most: the scripts and tests of this base use them with 22.18 | An HTTP client, an argument parser, a test runner |
| `require()` of an ES module | 22.12 | CommonJS and ES module workarounds |
| `Temporal` | 26 | A date library |

[S4] [S2] [S5]

## Sources

- [S1] Node.js, "Releases", <https://nodejs.org/en/about/previous-releases>, read 2026-11-12, MIT, summarized.
- [S2] Angular Team, "Version compatibility", v22, <https://angular.dev/reference/versions>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S3] Node.js, "Modules: TypeScript", v26, <https://nodejs.org/api/typescript.html>, read 2026-11-12, MIT, summarized.
- [S4] Node.js, "Node.js 22 is now available!", <https://nodejs.org/en/blog/announcements/v22-release-announce>, and "Node.js 26.0.0", <https://nodejs.org/en/blog/release/v26.0.0>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.

# RxJS 7 and Angular signals

Contents: 1. RxJS stays at 7 · 2. Deprecated in RxJS 7 · 3. Signals and observables · 4. The bridge · Sources

## 1. RxJS stays at 7 (RxJS versions)

The current version is 7.8.2. Version 8 is paused, with no release date: write RxJS 7 code. Angular 16 to 22 accept `^6.5.3 || ^7.4.0`. [S1] [S2]

## 2. Deprecated in RxJS 7 (RxJS 7 deprecations)

| Old pattern | Write instead |
| --- | --- |
| `source$.toPromise()` | `await firstValueFrom(source$)` or `await lastValueFrom(source$)` |
| `subscribe(next, error, complete)` | `subscribe({ next, error, complete })` |
| `Observable.create(…)` | `new Observable(…)` |

[S1]

Wrong:

```ts
const valves = await this.http.get<unknown>('/api/valves').toPromise();
```

Right:

```ts
const valves = await firstValueFrom(this.http.get<unknown>('/api/valves'));
```

## 3. Signals for state, observables for streams (Angular signals and RxJS)

- State of a component, and values derived from it: signals (`signal`, `computed`). [S3]
- Events over time, cancellation, combination of streams (messages, timers, sockets): observables. [S3]
- An observable in a template: the `async` pipe, or `toSignal()` in the component.
- A simple HTTP request, from Angular 22: `httpResource()`. Before 22: `HttpClient`.

## 4. The bridge between signals and observables (rxjs-interop)

| API, from `@angular/core/rxjs-interop` | Role | To know |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `toSignal(obs$, { initialValue })` | Observable to signal | Subscribes at once; needs the injection context, so call it in a field; without `initialValue`, gives `undefined` until the first value |
| `toObservable(signal)` | Signal to observable | Several synchronous writes give one value only |
| `rxResource({ params, stream })` | Resource fed by an observable | Same state signals as `resource` |
| `takeUntilDestroyed()` | End a subscription with the component | Without an argument, only in a field or the constructor |

[S3]

## Sources

- [S1] ReactiveX, types of `rxjs` 7.8.2 (`@deprecated` tags), <https://app.unpkg.com/rxjs@7.8.2/files/dist/types/internal/Observable.d.ts>, and the roadmap issue 6367, <https://github.com/ReactiveX/rxjs/issues/6367>, read 2026-11-12, Apache 2.0, summarized.
- [S2] Angular Team, "Version compatibility", v22, <https://angular.dev/reference/versions>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S3] Angular Team, "RxJS interop with Angular signals", v22, <https://angular.dev/ecosystem/rxjs-interop>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.

# TypeScript 5.0 to 7.0: what changed

Contents: 1. Version rule · 2. Syntax from 5.0 to 5.9 · 3. TypeScript 6.0 · 4. TypeScript 7.0 · Sources

## 1. Version rule (TypeScript versions)

Read the version of `typescript` in `package.json`. Use a syntax only when that version is equal to or higher than the version that added it. [S1]

In an Angular 22 project, `typescript` stays at 6.0: Angular 22 and typescript-eslint 8 accept `>=6.0.0 <6.1.0`. Never move it to 7 when you update dependencies. [S2] [S4]

## 2. Syntax added from 5.0 to 5.9 (TypeScript 5)

| Version | Use | Breaking change to know |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 5.0 | Standard decorators; `const` type parameters; `extends` with an array in `tsconfig`; `moduleResolution: bundler`; `verbatimModuleSyntax`; `export type *` | `target: ES3`, `out`, `importsNotUsedAsValues` deprecated |
| 5.2 | `using` and `await using` for disposable resources | `module` and `moduleResolution` must match for `node16` and `nodenext` |
| 5.3 | Import attributes: `import data from './a.json' with { type: 'json' }` | `super.x` on an instance field is an error |
| 5.4 | `NoInfer<T>`; narrowing kept in closures; `Object.groupBy`; `module: preserve` | Stricter assignment between enums |
| 5.5 | Inferred type predicates: `values.filter((v) => v !== undefined)` narrows the type | Options deprecated in 5.0 have no effect |
| 5.6 | Conditions always true or always false are errors; iterator helper methods | `.tsbuildinfo` always written |
| 5.8 | `erasableSyntaxOnly`: only syntax that Node.js can strip | `assert` in an import is refused under `nodenext` |
| 5.9 | `import defer`; `module: node20` | `ArrayBuffer` is no longer a supertype of `Buffer` |

[S1]

## 3. TypeScript 6.0 (released 2026-03-23)

New defaults: `strict: true`, `module: esnext`, `target` the latest ECMAScript (`es2025`), `types: []`, `rootDir` the folder of `tsconfig.json`, `noUncheckedSideEffectImports: true`. [S3]

| Old option or syntax | State in 6.0 | Write instead |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `"moduleResolution": "node"` or `"node10"` | Deprecated | `bundler` or `nodenext` |
| `"moduleResolution": "classic"`, `"outFile"` | Removed | `bundler`; ES modules |
| `"baseUrl"` | Deprecated | Full paths in `paths` |
| `"target": "es5"`, `"downlevelIteration"` | Deprecated | ES2015 at least |
| `"module": "amd"`, `"umd"`, `"systemjs"`, `"none"` | Not supported | ES modules |
| `module Foo { }` | Error | `namespace Foo { }` |
| `import x from './a.json' assert { type: 'json' }` | Error | `with { type: 'json' }` |

- To silence the deprecations while you migrate: `"ignoreDeprecations": "6.0"`. Remove it once done. [S3]
- New types: `Temporal`, `Map.prototype.getOrInsert`, `RegExp.escape`. [S3]

## 4. TypeScript 7.0 (released 2026-07-08)

- The compiler is native: builds are 7 to 12 times faster. `npm install -D typescript` installs it. [S4]
- It has no programming API before 7.1: Angular and typescript-eslint cannot use it yet. [S4]
- The options deprecated in 6.0 are errors. [S4]
- The 6.0 compiler stays available as `@typescript/typescript6`, command `tsc6`. [S4]

## Sources

- [S1] Microsoft, "TypeScript 5.0" to "TypeScript 5.9" release notes, <https://typescriptlang.org/docs/handbook/release-notes/typescript-5-0.html> and the pages of the following versions, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S2] Angular Team, "Version compatibility", v22, <https://angular.dev/reference/versions>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S3] D. Rosenwasser, "Announcing TypeScript 6.0", 2026-03-23, <https://devblogs.microsoft.com/typescript/announcing-typescript-6-0/>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S4] D. Rosenwasser, "Announcing TypeScript 7.0", 2026-07-08, <https://devblogs.microsoft.com/typescript/announcing-typescript-7-0/>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.

# AGENTS.project.md

The rules of this project. The base never writes this file: an update of the base keeps it as it
is. Keep it short: 40 lines at most, English, short imperative sentences.

## Tools and versions

- Angular 22, TypeScript 6.0, Node.js 24 in the dev container.
- .NET 10 for the server; C# 14.
- Kubernetes 1.36 on the cluster; Gateway API, no Ingress.

## Commands

| Task | Command |
| --- | --- |
| Run the server locally | `dotnet run --project src/Server` |
| Run the user interface | `npm start` |

## Conventions

- Name a feature folder after the equipment family, in kebab-case.
- Put the synoptic symbol classes in `src/app/synoptics/symbols/`.
- Validate manifests with the Kubernetes schemas of version 1.36.

# syntax=docker/dockerfile:1
# check=error=true
# Image of an Angular application served as static files. Copy as Dockerfile next to nginx.conf,
# replace "web" with the project name of angular.json, and set the versions of the project.

ARG NODE_VERSION=24
ARG NGINX_VERSION=1.29

FROM docker.io/library/node:${NODE_VERSION} AS build
WORKDIR /app
COPY package.json package-lock.json ./
RUN npm ci
COPY . .
RUN npm run build

FROM docker.io/nginx/nginx-unprivileged:${NGINX_VERSION} AS final

COPY --from=build /app/dist/web/browser /usr/share/nginx/html

USER 101
EXPOSE 8080

# Deployment and Service of an API. Replace the name "api", the image, the resources and the probe
# timings with the values of the project.
apiVersion: apps/v1
kind: Deployment
metadata:
  name: api
  labels:
    app.kubernetes.io/name: api
spec:
  replicas: 2
  selector:
    matchLabels:
      app.kubernetes.io/name: api
  strategy:
    type: RollingUpdate
    rollingUpdate:
      maxUnavailable: 0
      maxSurge: 1
  template:
    metadata:
      labels:
        app.kubernetes.io/name: api
    spec:
      securityContext:
        runAsNonRoot: true
        seccompProfile:
          type: RuntimeDefault
      imagePullSecrets:
        - name: registry-pull
      containers:
        - name: api
          image: registry.example.internal/api:1.0.0
          ports:
            - containerPort: 8080
          startupProbe:
            httpGet:
              path: /healthz/live
              port: 8080
            periodSeconds: 5
            failureThreshold: 30
          livenessProbe:
            httpGet:
              path: /healthz/live
              port: 8080
            periodSeconds: 10
            failureThreshold: 3
          readinessProbe:
            httpGet:
              path: /healthz/ready
              port: 8080
            periodSeconds: 5
            failureThreshold: 3
          resources:
            requests:
              cpu: "1"
              memory: "512Mi"
            limits:
              cpu: "1"
              memory: "512Mi"
          securityContext:
            allowPrivilegeEscalation: false
            readOnlyRootFilesystem: true
            capabilities:
              drop: ["ALL"]
          volumeMounts:
            - name: tmp
              mountPath: /tmp
      volumes:
        - name: tmp
          emptyDir: {}
---
apiVersion: v1
kind: Service
metadata:
  name: api
  labels:
    app.kubernetes.io/name: api
spec:
  selector:
    app.kubernetes.io/name: api
  ports:
    - port: 80
      targetPort: 8080

=====BASE-EXPORT-1-571e463b6744===== FILE 1/1 754daa071462ae994ddbe85d89887790013a6c5ef01306085d505be932004cc8 RAW code/templates/containers/devcontainer/Dockerfile
# syntax=docker/dockerfile:1
# check=error=true
# Image of the .NET and Angular dev container. The coding assistant and the check tools run in it.
# Copy this folder as .devcontainer/ at the project root.

ARG DOTNET_VERSION=10.0
ARG NODE_VERSION=24

FROM docker.io/library/node:${NODE_VERSION} AS node

FROM mcr.microsoft.com/dotnet/sdk:${DOTNET_VERSION}

# Node.js and npm, taken from the official Node.js image.
COPY --from=node /usr/local/bin/node /usr/local/bin/
COPY --from=node /usr/local/lib/node_modules /usr/local/lib/node_modules
RUN ln -s ../lib/node_modules/npm/bin/npm-cli.js /usr/local/bin/npm \
 && ln -s ../lib/node_modules/npm/bin/npx-cli.js /usr/local/bin/npx

# Check tools: put the Linux binaries of hadolint, kubeconform, kube-linter and helm in tools/ once.
COPY tools/ /usr/local/bin/

# Kubernetes JSON schemas for kubeconform, for the version of the cluster.
COPY kubernetes-schemas/ /opt/kubernetes-schemas/

# The coding assistant: its npm package with its version, "<package>@<version>", set in devcontainer.json.
ARG ASSISTANT_PACKAGE=""
# hadolint ignore=DL3016
RUN if [ -n "$ASSISTANT_PACKAGE" ]; then npm install --global "$ASSISTANT_PACKAGE"; fi

# Tools of the skill read-heavy-file: Poppler for PDF files, Tesseract for OCR in English and
# French. Set EXTRACT_TOOLS to false in devcontainer.json when apt packages cannot be installed.
ARG EXTRACT_TOOLS=true
# hadolint ignore=DL3008
RUN if [ "$EXTRACT_TOOLS" = "true" ]; then \
      apt-get update \
      && apt-get install --yes --no-install-recommends \
        poppler-utils tesseract-ocr tesseract-ocr-eng tesseract-ocr-fra \
      && rm -rf /var/lib/apt/lists/*; \
    fi

# Non-root user of the .NET images (UID 1654).
USER app

# syntax=docker/dockerfile:1
# check=error=true
# Image of an ASP.NET Core API. Copy as Dockerfile, then replace "Api" with the project name
# and set DOTNET_VERSION from TargetFramework (net10.0 gives 10.0).

ARG DOTNET_VERSION=10.0

FROM mcr.microsoft.com/dotnet/sdk:${DOTNET_VERSION} AS build
WORKDIR /source

# Copy the project file first: the restore layer stays cached while it does not change.
COPY Api/Api.csproj Api/
RUN dotnet restore Api/Api.csproj

# Copy the rest of the source, then publish.
COPY Api/ Api/
RUN dotnet publish Api/Api.csproj -c Release -o /app --no-restore

FROM mcr.microsoft.com/dotnet/aspnet:${DOTNET_VERSION} AS final
WORKDIR /app
COPY --from=build /app ./
USER $APP_UID
EXPOSE 8080
ENTRYPOINT ["dotnet", "Api.dll"]

# Static server of an Angular application, unprivileged NGINX on port 8080.
server {
  listen 8080;
  root /usr/share/nginx/html;
  index index.html;

  # Every unknown route returns index.html: Angular routing handles it.
  location / {
    try_files $uri $uri/ /index.html;
  }
}

=====BASE-EXPORT-1-571e463b6744===== FILE 1/1 f1ca2479dc0d7180411b94d91465daa05aee3bcbedfe194e089cff6b64c44862 RAW code/templates/csharp/format-csharp.sh
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Format C# code, or check its formatting with --check.
# One line of Directory.Build.props selects the formatter:
#   <StrictFormatting>true</StrictFormatting>   CSharpier, with code-style and analyzer fixes
#   <StrictFormatting>false</StrictFormatting>  dotnet format
# Run it from the folder that holds Directory.Build.props and the project or solution file.
# Strict mode needs CSharpier once: dotnet new tool-manifest; dotnet tool install csharpier
set -euo pipefail

mode="fix"
if [[ "${1:-}" == "--check" ]]; then
  mode="check"
fi

strict="false"
if grep -q "<StrictFormatting>true</StrictFormatting>" Directory.Build.props; then
  strict="true"
fi

if [[ "$strict" == "true" && "$mode" == "fix" ]]; then
  dotnet format style --no-restore
  dotnet format analyzers --no-restore
  dotnet csharpier format .
elif [[ "$strict" == "true" ]]; then
  dotnet csharpier check .
  dotnet format style --no-restore --verify-no-changes
  dotnet format analyzers --no-restore --verify-no-changes
elif [[ "$mode" == "fix" ]]; then
  dotnet format --no-restore
else
  dotnet format --no-restore --verify-no-changes
fi

=====BASE-EXPORT-1-571e463b6744===== FILE 1/1 0db5d88d059b77ea8b45645deb2d5023023331961daf3c901e855099f7705334 RAW code/templates/typescript-angular/synoptic-symbol.ts
// A reusable synoptic symbol, as docs/synoptics/symbol-component.md describes: a pure function
// turns the inputs into data attributes, one state word and one accessible name. The styles pick
// the fill and the badge from the data attributes; one clock per view makes the badges flash.
export type PositionState = 'open' | 'closed' | 'opening' | 'closing' | 'intermediate' | 'unknown';
export type AlarmPriority = 'none' | 'caution' | 'warning' | 'alarm' | 'emergency';
export type AlarmState = 'normal' | 'unacknowledged' | 'acknowledged';

export interface ValveSymbolInputs {
  readonly tagName: string;
  readonly positionState: PositionState;
  readonly actualPositionPercent?: number;
  readonly alarmPriority: AlarmPriority;
  readonly alarmState: AlarmState;
  readonly isOutOfService: boolean;
}

export interface SymbolView {
  readonly attributes: Readonly<Record<string, string>>;
  readonly stateText: string;
  readonly badgeLetter: string;
  readonly isBadgeFlashing: boolean;
  readonly accessibleName: string;
}

const POSITION_TEXT: Readonly<Record<PositionState, string>> = {
  open: 'OPEN',
  closed: 'CLOSED',
  opening: 'OPENING',
  closing: 'CLOSING',
  intermediate: 'PARTIAL',
  unknown: '?',
};
const BADGE_LETTER: Readonly<Record<AlarmPriority, string>> = {
  none: '',
  caution: 'C',
  warning: 'W',
  alarm: 'A',
  emergency: 'E',
};

function stateTextOf(inputs: ValveSymbolInputs): string {
  if (inputs.positionState === 'intermediate' && inputs.actualPositionPercent !== undefined) {
    return `${String(Math.round(inputs.actualPositionPercent))} %`;
  }
  return POSITION_TEXT[inputs.positionState];
}

export function toValveView(inputs: ValveSymbolInputs): SymbolView {
  const stateText = stateTextOf(inputs);
  const hasAlert = inputs.alarmPriority !== 'none';
  const alarmText = hasAlert ? `, ${inputs.alarmPriority} ${inputs.alarmState}` : '';

  return {
    attributes: {
      'data-position': inputs.positionState,
      'data-alarm-priority': inputs.alarmPriority,
      'data-alarm-state': inputs.alarmState,
      'data-out-of-service': String(inputs.isOutOfService),
    },
    stateText,
    badgeLetter: BADGE_LETTER[inputs.alarmPriority],
    isBadgeFlashing: hasAlert && inputs.alarmState === 'unacknowledged',
    accessibleName: `Valve ${inputs.tagName}, ${stateText}${alarmText}`,
  };
}

// One clock per view: it sets data-flash-phase on the view root, so that every badge flashes in
// phase, at 1 Hz by default. Call the returned function when the view closes.
export function startFlashClock(root: HTMLElement, periodMs = 1000): () => void {
  if (window.matchMedia('(prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)').matches) {
    root.dataset['flashPhase'] = 'on';
    return () => undefined;
  }
  let isOn = true;
  const timer = window.setInterval(() => {
    isOn = !isOn;
    root.dataset['flashPhase'] = isOn ? 'on' : 'off';
  }, periodMs / 2);

  return () => {
    window.clearInterval(timer);
  };
}
=====BASE-EXPORT-1-571e463b6744===== END

=====BASE-EXPORT-1-571e463b6744===== FILE 1/1 fcd9bff138bbf2adf7731d8e0c4a3720c37b12aa177ec40c8748bc35dcae95d7 RAW chat/models/commit-assistant.md
---
id: commit-assistant
name: Commit assistant
description: Proposes a commit message in the Conventional Commits format, and nothing else.
subject: commit messages
scope: restricted
knowledge: none
skills: write-commit-message
access: team
---

# Commit assistant

You are the commit message assistant of a team. Some users are not developers: use plain words.

## Rules

1. If the request is not about a commit message, refuse in one sentence and say what you can do.
2. You can do three things: propose a commit message, explain the type you chose, fix a message the user pastes.
3. Ask what changed and why, if the user did not say it. Ask one short question at a time.
4. Follow the skill write-commit-message for the format of the message.
5. Write the commit message in English. Talk to the user in the language of the question, never a mix of two languages.
6. Never invent a change. Use only what the user says.
7. You cannot run commands or make the commit. Never say that you did. The user copies the message.
8. Never search the web. Never open a URL.

## Answer format

1. The commit message in a `text` code block.
2. One sentence: the type you chose and why.
3. If the change does two things, two messages, one per commit.
